# RESEARCH LEDGER — TubeTracker tracking attempts (consolidated)

Purpose: one place that records everything tried — by Hermes, by prior
Claude sessions ("fable" et al.), and what came of it. Read this BEFORE
proposing anything new. Update it AFTER every new attempt.

The ledger preserves the findings from retired iterations. Their superseded
source and generated review artifacts were removed during repository cleanup;
the maintained v17 and v18 implementations and the isolated v19 crossing study
remain under `prototypes/`.

Ground truth about the biology first (it constrains every tracker):

## How these tubes actually grow (measured / observed)
- Pollen-tube tip growth is **vesicle-mediated extension at a single apical
  point**: only the last few µm extend; the shank is rigid.
- Growth is **pulsatile** on ~10²–10³ s scales but frame-to-frame it is
  SLOW and SMOOTH: ≤ ~7.5 px per sampled frame ever observed; typical
  steps are sub-pixel to a few px.
- The tip trajectory is **coherent in path coordinates**: arclength s(t)
  is monotone non-decreasing and smooth; lateral offset n(t) wanders
  slowly (tube sways as a whole with the grain, 10–20 px over long spans,
  but that is RIGID-BODY sway of the whole tube, not tip thrash).
- Therefore any tracker whose per-frame tip moves > ~3 px median while
  "growing" is modeling noise, not biology. This is the acceptance gate.

### Hermes bake-off (2026-08-21, prototypes/ v1) — EVAL.md round 1
All four shared the SAME weakness later identified by the user: identity/
length was optimized while TIP COHERENCE was ignored. Length numbers were
"won" with tips that jump 20+ px/frame — biologically absurd.

## Hard-won cross-cutting lessons (do not re-learn)
1. Coordinates: cached tracks are in the 960-wide analysis frame; old caches
   store centers in crop coords — load_body_tracks handles both.
2. CoTracker landmarks drift silently (47–76px, full visibility); rotation
   about grain center is invisible to center-based checks.
3. Single-frame evidence buries faint tubes (evidence ≈1–3); temporal-median
   (±8) is the minimum SNR floor for detection.
4. Absolute thresholds don't transfer across videos — gates must self-
   calibrate against measured on-tube statistics.
5. **Tip coherence is a FIRST-CLASS metric**, equal to recovery. A tracker
   that wins length while thrashing the tip has lost.

## Open directions (ranked, unexplored or partially explored)
0. **BREAKTHROUGH CONCEPT — Growth-Consistent Material Tomography**
   (see `prototypes/BREAKTHROUGH.md`): stop tracking, start reconstructing.
   The tube is a static structure progressively revealed; the whole video
   is ONE estimation problem with a monotone-set prior (per-pixel PAVA in
   time), pooled consensus evidence (v4's validated gray test, quantiled
   over time), and length as a geodesic READOUT on the consensus map.
   Formulation-level answer to all 15 ledger failures; concept only by
   user instruction.
1. **Material-stable kymograph columns** — H8 makes this top priority for
   ONSET timing; v4 solved the LENGTH side (gray gating), onset timing
   still needs material-stable columns.
2. **v4.5: v4 + temporal direction coherence (from H15's angle-DP) +
   SCUE bands (H14)** — the synthesis of everything that worked: curved
   corridor from v4, support semantics from v4, direction-consistency
   check between consecutive frames, and uncertainty bands via margin
   sweep. This is the nearest-term strong tracker.
3. **Crossing-specific validation**: find an isolated crossing event,
   annotate truth, test there.
4. SAM2 retry with proper prompting + light fine-tune on existing labels.
5. Learned point proposals (cnn_prototype scaffolding exists; labels exist).
6. Joint multi-tube ILP (H1's association engine) with v4-style gray gates
   per tube — crossing resolution + honest lengths together.
| H29 | **v15 Motion-Basis Instance Binding** (`v15_mbb/`): the physics-based identity answer for dense fields — every grain is a rigid body; its tube inherits that motion at the root; per-pixel argmin over per-grain rigid-motion bases + static basis = instance label. Ran on P0034: 19 grains, all motions estimated (ECC), binding verdict: **tracked position binds STATIC — no grain's rigid motion explains flow there**. Motion census: ALL objects (grains, S-tube, debris blob) share common-mode drift ~1-1.8px/10f; differential motion below ECC noise floor ⇒ **P0034's field is effectively static + global drift; motion-based binding has no signal to bind with**. Vision + geometry: the thick S-tube near the tracked position hangs from ~(457,175), 21.5px from the tracked point, extending to (483,289) — no blob-detector grain at its top end (nearest 148px). The scene's "grains" in the census are mostly OUT-OF-FOCUS bodies; the tracked object is small/dim. **Verdict: P0034 high-density identity cannot be resolved by motion (no differential motion) NOR by appearance (all tubes identical) NOR by connectivity (one merged dark web, 85k px). The honest answer stands: needs either higher-magnification footage or user adjudication.** Broad lesson recorded: motion-basis binding works when sources MOVE INDEPENDENTLY; it degrades gracefully to "static" verdict when they don't — which is itself diagnostic. | negative with mechanism + broad-lesson |
| H30 | **THE P0034 RESOLUTION — the recording is a 2-HOUR TIMELAPSE** (`movie1_overview.jpg`, `tracked_grain_timeline.jpg`). P0034's 240 analyzed frames sample src frames 0→104901 of Pollen tube movie 1 (105192 frames @14fps) every ~438 src frames = **31 seconds per analysis frame**. The full-movie overview shows: bare grains at src f0-9.5k → **germination begins ~src f10-19k** → continuous growth through f105k → a giant out-of-focus blob drifts in from src ~28k and increasingly obscures the field. The tracked grain's OWN S-tube (the one v15's proximity binding found) **grows on camera between src f26k-35k = analysis frames 60-80** — the kymograph along its path shows background (177) until f60, tube levels (106-158) arriving across the 60-720px section by f80. **The v14 germination detection at analysis-f38 was measuring a focus/illumination event, but the REAL germination of this tube is analysis-f~65-75 (src ~29-33k).** Final S-tube arclength ~720px of new material (60-720px sections) + pre-existing crossings in the first 180px. Why every per-frame tracker failed here: at 31s/frame, a tube growing 0.2px/s jumps ~40px BETWEEN analyzed frames — sequential tracking is meaningless at this cadence; only timelapse-aware readouts (birth-time per section, like BTM/CED) are valid. The "phantom corridor" verdict (H20) stands — v4's corridor was never on this tube — but the tube ITSELF is real and now measured: **P0034 true answer ≈ 720px grown over analysis frames 60-80 (src 26k-35k)**, video `P0034_TRUE_growth_fnum.mp4`. | RESOLVED: timelapse cadence was the missing context |
| H31 | **DENSE-WINDOW PARADIGM VALIDATED + FIELD ATLAS** (`v15_mbb/extract_dense_window.py`, `dense_readout.py`). Re-sampled movie 1 at 3s cadence (1886 frames, src 26k→105k, `runs/cache/P0034_dense/`, 1.3GB). Results on the S-tube region: **the S-tube grows ON CAMERA in 36 seconds** (dense d22→d30, src ~27k): absent at d16-22, a segment from the TOP-LEFT grain at d24, full S-curve by d26-28. It belongs to the **top-left grain, NOT the tracked one** — it merely passes the tracked grain. The tracked grain's OWN stub (~13px, pointing left-up) predates the window and **stalls permanently** (gray 155→147, no extension through d400). Deliverables: `birth_time_map.jpg` (color=germination time), `atlas_timelapse.mp4` (every tube colored by component as it appears), `atlas_components.json`, `born_in_window.json`. **THE PARADIGM: for timelapse-scale footage, the unit of analysis is not the trajectory but the BIRTH EVENT — per-section birth times over the whole field (who, when, where, how fast). Trajectory tracking applies only when cadence resolves growth (≥1 frame per 10px of growth).** Cadence rule of thumb: analysis interval must be ≤ growth-duration/20. | paradigm validated, atlas delivered |

Run on all three datasets: P0034 coarse (activity at coarse-bins 0/4/196/200/172), P0034-dense fine (93 components, biggest durations hours-scale; 3s interval = trajectory-valid ✓), lowdens fine (37 components; **the tracked tube's construction duration = 142s → cadence law demands ≤7.1s intervals; the original 31s analysis is ATLAS-ONLY**). **THE REFRAME THIS FORCES: v14's lowdens "tracking" success was actually an atlas success — its birth-event readout (annulus CUSUM + construction births) is atlas machinery that happens to match v4 because BOTH measure the developed front of a single-tube atlas.** Growth direction measured: tube built from (337,118) toward (350,18) — upward, consistent with the visible tube. Deliverables: runs/prototypes/v16/{P0034_coarse,P0034_dense_fine,lowdens1_P0016_coarse,lowdens_fine}/report.json + birth_fine.npy + birth_time_map_fine.jpg. | refined prototype delivered; cadence law quantified |

(2) cached timing multiplied source-frame spacing twice: P0034-dense's 6 s fine samples were reported as 252 s, producing the impossible 35,532 s component duration. (3) v16's “biggest component” was often border/focus clutter, not a tube. (4) the stored `s_tube_path.npy` used to support the ~720 px P0034 claim is **841.7 px inside a 44×93 px box**, with endpoint chord 83 px and tortuosity ~10; visual inspection shows it loops over tube walls rather than tracing one centerline. The known low-density P0016 path retained correct geometry at 34.728 analysis px, approximately 69.5 px at the historical 960-wide scale, but its median phase timing disagreement was about 69 seconds on a fixed path and 39 seconds after translating the entire path with pollen motion. Inspection showed that `temporal_link_samples`, a component-linking horizon, also permitted each front to infer construction up to 192 seconds before optical confirmation. Independent fits could therefore assign very different times while satisfying the same broad bounds. Whole-path pollen translation also reduced support on unrelated controls. No loosened time gate was accepted. | geometry consensus is not timing consensus; global translation rejected |
| H74 | **Branch-locked nonrigid registration improves alignment but does not solve timing by itself.** A fixed v17 topology now permits per-point offsets shaped as `(time, path point, xy)`. Pollen displacement is applied at the root and smoothly fades over the proximal shank; interior points move only along local path normals inside a configurable 3 px corridor. Spatial and temporal median regularization, a per-sample normal-step cap, root locking, and birth-aware activation make branch switching structurally unavailable. Synthetic tests recover a bending tube that fixed and rigid paths miss. On P0016, alternate-phase path coverage reached 96.9% and median timing disagreement improved from 81.0 seconds fixed to 51.0 seconds branch-locked, but no trajectory passed the time-domain gate. The deformation is retained for rendering and evidence sampling, while its timing result is recorded as negative. | useful registration layer; not a timing solution |

This proves the large time disagreement is not only the former 192-second inference horizon: at the observed growth rate, subpixel-to-one-pixel localization differences map to tens of seconds. The visible-front estimator is retained, but pointwise arrival time is not used as the trajectory acceptance metric. | direct timing is more honest; seconds remain the wrong stability domain |

Timing disagreement remains exported as uncertainty and exact germination onset still requires time-domain cue agreement. The selected P0016 winner is 36.314 px at width 480, equivalent to **72.627 px at width 960**, matching the user's approximately 72 px visual reference. Its two phase trajectories agree within 1.5 px for 94.6% of matched times, with 1.0 px median, 1.414 px p90, and 3.0 px maximum disagreement despite a 45-second median node-arrival difference. | H296 | **Selection blocker: the junk-proposal classes named by eye, and a relative image-evidence gate that recovers 1/8 and halves tip error (causally isolated).** The v30 route-duel round (8 winner-vs-rival tasks mined from `movie_v17`, judged by the user) returned 5 "neither" + 3 preferences with one field observation: candidates "go in loops, or obviously incorrect straight lines." The candidate table confirms both classes exactly. The `a*` attachment family is 25-31 of ~35 candidates per event and is literally 2-point straight 120px lines with no image content; the model's top pick was a fan in 3/8 events (p01 `a1d0l120r18` tip 0.387; p02 `a6d0l120r18` 0.656 with the top 9 slots all fans 0.66->0.28; dt-007 `a1d0l120r8` 0.908 with fans in the top 7 up to 0.994). Where a walker faced a fan the user chose the walker (p01->B, p02->B); the shipped winner was right in 1/8 (p04). The trained ranker is engaged and anti-correlated with the front softmax (p04: top-tip `p0c+` route_p 0.000 vs winner 0.525; dt-004: 0.028 vs 0.623), so the shipped rule `q_max x route_p` is what lets a saturating front confidence outvote the ranker. Every candidate of the 6 gold events was re-scored offline (`prototypes/v30_video_apex/eval_route_evidence.py`; frame-normalized H148 wall support along each route, dense resample so 2-point lines are scored, not trivially excluded): evidence alone is a bad selector (0/6 - it re-ranks toward any high-contrast edge, picking foreign tubes at coverage 0.0), but evidence as a RELATIVE gate (>= 0.6 x event-best) before the ranker's vote selects a route-correct candidate in 4/6 events including BOTH dev events (p01: shipped 0.0 -> gated 0.5; p04 right), versus 3/6 for route_p alone and 2/6 for the shipped `sel`. Wired as `scripts/run_v30_movie.py --select-evidence-gate FLOAT` (default 0.0 = behavior-preserving; the gate restricts selection only, every candidate stays exported with `evidence.wall_ev` and `gate_kept`, and the flag is recorded in measurements and report). End-to-end on the 8 events: `movie_v17` (snap13) and the gate=0 control (snap15) are winner-identical (0/8, median 73.0px, p90 117.0px) which proves the snapshot relocation is behavior-neutral; gate=0.6 gives selected 1/8, median 33.4px, p90 79.8px, oracle unchanged 5/8, kept-pool 6-21 of 29-40 (3 events changed winner: dt-007 fan->walker, p01 fan->fan, p02 fan->walker = the realized hit). Same round, non-selection items: 20 fresh apex labels banked into `runs/prototypes/timesfm/tip_dataset_v7demo` (13 human tips with task/obs/revision lineage verified, all frame images present; the 7 ball-scoped no-tube verdicts remain inert by the no-invented-negatives rule and want ball-scoped negative discs); the v30 snapshot lineage was moved OFF /tmp to `runs/prototypes/v30/snapshots/{snap14,snap15}` after the /tmp wipe was found to have already destroyed `annotator_big` (12 traces, 6 raster masks, 8 duels, 4 census tiles) while 8/9 project DBs survived under `~/Documents/New project/TubeTracker-rev7-audit-2026-09-11/annotation_databases/`. Honest limits: 8 events / 6 with human routes; the 0.6 factor is provisional and only self-calibrating per event, not universal; the headline metric is tip distance <= 5px (strict) while the route-level gain is larger; and training on the folded duel verdicts (snap15 now feeds duels.json + `--hard-routes`) has not been run. | junk-classes named; a subtractive, evidence-relative gate is a real (if modest) selection gain, causally isolated; fan-family eligibility was the missing piece, not more negatives |
| H297 | **rev8 step 3: the crop-scale encoder, matched prompts, and the whole-instance harness (verified numbers only).** (1) **Architecture.** The review measured the body head's receptive field at 9x9px and concluded a grain/attachment prompt cannot establish distant tube connectivity through it; measured here on the real classes by gradient support: rev7 = **8px**, new `_MultiScaleUNet` (4 stride-2 levels, dilated bottleneck 2/4/8, mirrored skips, owner channel re-injected at EVERY decoder level) = **287px** on a 288 crop. The hinge test: gradient of a distal body pixel w.r.t. the prompt region is exactly **0.0** for rev7 and non-zero for the multiscale encoder — i.e. the distal answer CAN now depend on the query at all (before it could not, structurally). `--multiscale` selects it; old checkpoints are not shape-compatible, and the 1x1-spatial temporal conv becomes 3x3x3 in that variant. (2) **Matched prompts.** Deployment scores candidates with an automatic grain disc (`provenance="auto-grain"`, H291); training saw only human root geometry, so the head could learn to expect a hint no deployment provides. `--prompt-mix` now draws per query from auto-grain/human/none (default 0.4/0.4/0.2, seeded), the dev probe reads the DEPLOYMENT prompt, and `label_usage.json` records the declared mix and the counts actually drawn (smoke: 42/49/12). (3) **Withheld regions produce zero loss** (exit criterion): three tests on the real builders/loss — no gradient at any pixel outside the valid mask, the loss value is invariant to targets outside it, and a scoped verdict stays bounded (`APEX_NEGATIVE_SCOPES`={apex,tip,cap}); a scope outside that set is ignored, never widened. (4) **Bug found in the body readout:** the dev IoU's union was `p | (t & m)` (operator precedence) so predictions sprawling outside the reviewed extent inflated the denominator and silently deflated every body number; replaced by `mask_iou_split` (union masked on BOTH sides) which also splits proximal/distal at 40px from the queried grain and reports `painted_distal_frac` so a stub tube cannot flatter a distal score (4 tests). (5) **Data/contract fixes that the new annotation forced, each with a test:** mask entities now carry the queried object (`target_xy`/`target_r`) and the snapshot backfills it from the task — without it an evaluation cannot rebuild the deployment prompt; a ball-scoped "no tube at this grain" verdict exists end-to-end (UI button + `save_mask_none`, empty paint, `scope="ball"`; the snapshot turns it into a CAP-scoped negative disc at the queried grain only, never a frame negative, H242); the duplicate guard now keys on (movie, frame, owner, **kind**) because one owner at one frame legitimately carries several heads of supervision while a same-kind repeat stays a refusal. (6) **Snapshots.** `scripts/rebuild_v30_snapshot.sh` rebuilds from the PARENT manifest's own source list plus named new projects, with `--default-movie` (the earlier rebuild omitted it and dropped the movie mapping on 63 records — 71 observations with an empty `movie_path`, which crashed the trainer at load; now 0). snap17 = snap16 + the 3 extents-carrying masks (7 masks, 3 linked, 3 with extents, 0 rows removed); snap18 = snap17 + 3 paired masks. (7) **Neighbouring tubes (the "few neighbouring tubes" the brief asks for).** FRST (`find_neighbor_balls.py`) found a clump of 3 strong grains at ld:42000 76-166px apart; `build_v30pair_batch.py` built 3 mask tasks sharing ONE view centre with a magenta target ring (a 2-corner ellipse renders as a view-covering blob in napari 0.9.1 — reported by the user, replaced with an explicit closed circle path); the user painted all three (298/548/385px, complete, extents recorded) and they are genuinely distinct tubes: **zero pairwise overlap**, centroids 76/92/166px apart. (8) **Swap harness** (`eval_whole_instance.py`): rebuilds the crop and 9-frame clip exactly as training does (QUERY_OFFSETS, `load_clip_pixels`, /255 grayscale), holds the crop fixed, swaps ONLY the owner query, and reports the response matrix plus diag/off-diag IoU, rows peaking on their own tube, and **max row spread** — the last is what exposes query-invariant output (identical rows => spread 0.0), tested against diagonal-dominant, invariant and partial matrices. Current numbers on the disjoint 1-epoch smoke (trained before the pairs existed): diag 0.036 / off-diag 0.000 / distal 0.00 / spread 0.060 with probabilities ~0.36 everywhere — the harness works; the model is untrained, so this is NOT a result. Same caveat on the smoke's dev heat: at 120 updates the heat field is flat (heat_at_truth 0.3535 vs max 0.3538, neg-region boxes at 99.95% of the crop max) so its "heat errors" are argmax noise, not localisation. Pending: the 6-epoch snap17 fit (multiscale, p05 frame held out) for distal body IoU on a held-out masked event, then the same swap matrix with that checkpoint. Suite 609. | step-3 machinery complete + verified; the learning result is the next number |
| H298 | **First fitted read is ZERO, and the reason is the loss, not the data.** The multiscale fit's ep1 checkpoint (240 updates on snap17, p05 frame held out) gives body IoU **0.00** on the held-out p05 mask AND every cell of the paired-grain swap matrix, with `max row spread 0.000` — i.e. the head predicts nothing above 0.5 anywhere in a 288 crop. Diagnosis: `body_valid` is a thin paint band plus a large reviewed extent, so valid pixels are ~99% background and a plain masked BCE's fastest descent is "predict nothing" (this is H273's base-rate state re-appearing at a bigger encoder). Fix per the brief's "balanced masked losses": per-sample `pos_weight = neg/pos` computed over VALID pixels only, clamped to 20x. Verified arithmetic (with p=0.5 the loss equals log2*(pos*w+neg)/n_val, tested for both the uncapped 1.25 ratio and the capped 20x case) and the withheld-region guarantee is re-asserted after balancing: pixels outside `body_valid` still receive exactly zero gradient. Relaunched the same fit (same seeds/settings, `--save-every 1`) as `front_rev8_fit2`; the previous partial run is kept as `front_rev8_fit_imbal_pt`. Suite 614. | balanced loss is the declared design; the swap matrix is the readout |
| H299 | **Why the body head could not learn the far end: the loss PREFERRED a low constant, and split normalization fixes it.** The 20-epoch multiscale run (fit3, base 8, 2200 updates) was a partial success with a precise failure: on the HELD-OUT p05 tube the body head reached whole-mask IoU 0.25-0.29 and proximal 0.36-0.49 (from 0.00), the heat field sharpened (neg-region box/max 0.996 -> 0.786), front error on held-out events fell to 3.7-7.8px in good epochs -- but **distal IoU was 0.0 in 15 of 20 epochs** (max 0.159). A new probability profile readout along the tube showed why: at 20-40px from the grain the head outputs a CONSTANT 0.456 whether the tube is present (paint 21%) or absent (40-60px, paint 2%) -- it never uses far-field image content. The discriminating experiment was the single-sample plumbing test (`--only-refs mask-rev8m-000`, 40 updates on ONE painted tube): proximal IoU **0.92**, distal **0.00** on the very tube it was memorising, with its profile flat (0.44) across bins that are **100% tube**. Arithmetic then named the cause: a tube is ~0.36% of its reviewed extent, so an extent-averaged masked BCE (even with a 20x pos_weight cap) has its minimum at a LOW CONSTANT (p=0.068) -- the far end could never win that comparison. Fix: split normalization for the body head, the convention the apex head already uses (P2) -- `0.5*(mean_BCE_pos + mean_BCE_neg)`, so each class normalizes on its own pixels, a constant is minimised at p=0.5, and per-class total gradient mass is equal by construction (5 tests: constant-minimum-at-0.5, count-independence, the gradient-mass identity, withheld-zero preserved, single-class masks cannot NaN). Re-run of the same plumbing test: held-out tubes now carry **distal IoU 0.174/0.159** (was 0.0) after ~20 single-sample epochs, and at 60 updates the four held-out mask rows read distal **0.159 / 0.174 / 0.323 / 0.325** (whole-mask 0.22-0.40) while the TRAINED tube fits at whole-mask **IoU 1.000** (was 0.46 = proximal 0.92 + distal 0.00). The head can express a whole instance; the loss was the blocker. Also fixed while here: the neg-region `box_to_max_ratio` divided by a near-zero heat max and printed values like -2.4e8 (arithmetic noise that looks like a finding); it now reports None with the crop max alongside when the max is non-positive. Suite 616. | loss structure, not supervision scale, was the blocker |
| H300 | **The mask query was 25-30px off the grain, and 60% of mask queries trained query-blind — the head learned "where tubes are", not "which tube is mine".** With the clump TRAINED (fit5, snap18, new split-normalized loss), the swap matrix came back with **identical rows**: diag 0.183 = off-diag 0.183, `max row spread 0.000`, 1/3 rows peaking on their own tube — the query-invariant signature, now with the clump in the training set. The disjoint control (fit4, snap17, clump held out) had the opposite shape but a tiny magnitude: 3/3 rows peak on their own tube, diag 0.055 vs off-diag 0.000, spread 0.076. Two geometry defects explain the trained run. (1) The AUTO-GRAIN prompt disc is drawn at `focus_xy`, and for a mask sample `focus_xy` was the PAINT CENTROID — on the paired masks that is **25-30px** from the queried grain (g0 centroid (536.7,670.1) vs grain (557,691)), so the disc landed on tube body instead of the grain, and the crop was framed on the tube's middle while deployment frames around the grain (the runner centres on the ball). (2) The prompt mix drew "human" for mask samples 40% of the time, but a mask has no attachment/proximal geometry (that comes from a traced observation), so those draws rendered ZEROS — with the 20% explicit "none", **60% of mask queries trained with no query at all**. Fixes, each with a test: mask samples anchor on `target_xy` (the grain) when the mask carries one, falling back to paint centroid/raster bbox; and a new `resolve_prompt_kind()` makes a drawn kind fall back to one the sample can actually render — only an explicit "none" may mean zeros. Verified: the paired masks now anchor at the grain, and a "human" draw on a mask sample becomes an auto-grain disc. Both prior runs were killed (they used the defective anchoring) and relaunched as fit6 (24 epochs, base 8, clump in train, p05 frame held out) so the swap test and the disjoint distal read come from one run. Suite 618. | prompt geometry, not capacity, is the current hypothesis; fit6 is the test |
| H301 | **Framing was the confound: with the query no longer always centred, the swap matrix flips from query-invariant to query-selective.** Added `--mask-jitter N` (seeded translated crops per body-mask sample, accepted only when the painted bbox stays inside the crop) because the swap test holds ONE crop fixed while the query moves — a model that only ever saw its query at the crop centre can read the framing instead of the query. Two runs, same snapshot/loss/seed/epochs, differ only in jitter: **fit6 (no jitter, 2 epochs)** gave identical rows (diag = off-diag = 0.183, `max row spread 0.000`, 1/3 rows self-peaked) and the same for fit5/ep3; **fit7 (`--mask-jitter 6`, 2 epochs)** gives **3/3 rows peaking on their own tube**, off-diag **0.000**, `max row spread 0.059` (query g0->g0 0.06, g1->g1 0.04, g2->g2 0.05 with every cross-cell 0.00). Also added a CONTROL query (`--query-at`, incl. `auto` = the mask-union centre, which lands on background between the tubes): with the centred runs the background query returned the SAME row as a grain query (IoU 0.155/0.199/0.195 vs 0.16/0.20/0.20) with a flat 0.500 profile — the definitive proof that the owner channel was being ignored, not merely under-weighted. With fit7 the grain rows are distinct and self-peaked, though the control still lights g1 somewhat (0.06), so selectivity is not yet clean and the magnitudes are small (0.04-0.06 IoU at 2 epochs). Honest read: the direction is right and causally isolated to the framing/jitter change, but this is a plumbing-scale result on 3 specimens, not yet the whole-instance claim. fit7 runs 24 epochs; the checkpoints are the readout. At 4 epochs (ep3) the structure STRENGTHENS: 3/3 rows self-peaked, off-diag 0.000, spread 0.081, and the held-out p05 tube reads IoU 0.24/proximal 0.34/distal 0.0 (distal lags here, as it did in fit3). Control placement mattered and is fixed: the mask-union CENTRE is not background for a clump — it landed inside g1's tube and returned a 0.29 response, which would have been read as a false positive; `--query-at auto` now picks the EMPTIEST crop point (farthest from any painted pixel), where the response is 0.000 on all three tubes. That gives the full hierarchy: grain query -> own tube only; background query -> nothing; identical rows -> query-invariant. | framing confound removed; diagonal-dominance appears and strengthens; small magnitudes, distal still pending |
| H302 | **The swap collapse has a price tag, and the loss was under-charging for it.** The base-4 run (snap18, jitter 6, 24 epochs, p05 frame held out, under `caffeinate` -- the machine sleeps between turns, which is why earlier jobs stalled) reached **distal IoU 0.16** on the held-out p05 tube by epoch 20 (whole-mask 0.22, proximal 0.34) but the swap matrix on the clump COLLAPSED BACK to identical rows (`max row spread 0.000`), with the background-query control returning the same row as a grain query (0.155/0.199/0.195 vs 0.16/0.20/0.20) -- the query-invariant shortcut, now with the distal half present. Arithmetic names it: in a crop holding three labelled tubes, predicting ALL of them costs the body loss ~0.003 while predicting only the queried one costs ~0.000, because the negative term averages over the whole crop where the other tubes are ~1% of the pixels. Wrong-instance activation was effectively free, so training was free to ignore the query. Fix: **confusable negatives** -- other LABELLED tubes in the same crop (decoded from the snapshot's masks at that frame, no invented geometry) carry `CONFUSABLE_W = 10x` weight in the body loss. Three tests: covering a neighbouring tube costs >1.5x more loss than covering none; the per-pixel negative weight on a confusable is exactly 1+10x a far-background pixel; and the withheld-region zero-gradient guarantee still holds exactly. Suite 622. A/B launched (`front_rev8_conf`, identical recipe to the base-4 run plus confusables, `caffeinate`, checkpoints every 4 epochs): the comparison is distal on the held-out p05 tube AND whether the clump swap rows stay distinct at 20 epochs. Also fixed this round, both my bugs, both reported by the user against the live app: the mask-task RING was falling back to the traced path's MEAN (for obs-v30t-004 that is **56px** from the ball) -- the builder now writes `target_xy` from the ball (fallback: the tube's grain exit), and the two live tasks were corrected in place; and the mask-task VIEW was centred on the tube's midpoint, so the ring sat off to the side -- tasks can now declare `view_zoom` (default 5.0, unchanged for every existing task) and both use 4.0 with the camera on the ringed grain. | distal won, swap lost to an under-priced shortcut; the fix is now in the loss and under A/B |
| H303 | **The confusable term was inert for a real reason, and fixing it moves the swap criterion for the first time.** Three nested causes, each found by measurement after the other: (1) the A/B arms came out **byte-identical** (`sha256` match on the two checkpoints), which proved the term never engaged; (2) instrumenting the trainer showed the confusable masks WERE built (120 of 128 body-mask sights, 83304 px) and reached the loss, so the fault was upstream of the arithmetic: the other tubes lie **outside that mask's reviewed extent**, where rev8 correctly declares material *unknown* (validity 0) — and `0 x weight = 0`. My weighting multiplied zero. Fixed by extending `body_valid` with the confusable region: another human-painted tube is not unknown material, it is known **not-my-tube** for this query (this is the same class of reasoning as H242: a verdict is scoped, and here the scope is "this query's tube"). (3) Even then, a 1-epoch A/B *cannot* detect the term: at that point the head's field is constant, the per-pixel BCE is identical everywhere, and a weighted mean of a constant equals the plain mean — my first test design was blind by construction (the same trap the constant-prediction unit test fell into). Tested properly at 8 epochs on the two paired grains, same data/seed/epochs, one difference: **weight 0 vs 10 gives different checkpoints** (`4abda4ec…` vs `d60eb847…`) and a materially different swap matrix — diag 0.183 / off-diag **0.183** / spread **0.000** / 1-of-3 rows self-peaked / control row lighting all three tubes (0.155/0.199/0.195) WITH the term off, versus diag 0.146 / off-diag **0.062** / spread **0.229** / **2-of-3** rows self-peaked / control row 0.155/0.199/**0.109** with it on. The off-diagonal (wrong-tube activation) falls ~3x and the rows stop being copies of each other, which is the whole content of the query-swap criterion; the honest limits are that the diagonal also drops slightly (0.183 -> 0.146), the background query still lights g0/g1 (the indiscriminate habit is weakened, not gone), and this is 8 epochs at base 4 on two trained grains. Suite 622. | criterion 2 moves for the first time, causally isolated |
| H304 | **The ring was anchored by a field whose meaning changes between observation families, and the builder wrote the key twice.** The user's screenshot showed the magenta ring enclosing empty background on a mask task, with the queried grain floating just outside it at the top of the frame. Cause, measured rather than guessed: (1) `build_v30mask_batch.py`'s task dict carried `target_xy` and `target_r` **twice**, so the last write silently won — the classic duplicate-key trap, invisible at review time; (2) the winning value came from `o["focus_xy"]` under the assumption that focus is the ball, but `focus_xy` is a **path point** for `obs-r4-p03` (a route observation) and the **ball** for `obs-v30t-004` (a pair observation, the same field `find_neighbor_balls.py` treats as the ball). Measured against FRST grain detections: p03's ring sat **46.2 px** down the tube from the nearest grain while its **path start sat 12.9 px** from the grain centre — i.e. on the grain's edge, because a path begins where the tube leaves the grain, so 14 px ring radius covers the grain either way; v30t-004's ring was 2.2 px from its grain, i.e. already correct. Fix: `ring_anchor_for_observation()` — explicit ball field first, then `focus_xy` **only when it lies within `target_r` of the path start**, else the path start. Applied in place to both live tasks (rev8m2-000 ring moves 356.5,432.7 -> 351.5,399.8) and pinned by `tests/test_ring_anchor.py` (5 tests, including that the threshold is the ring radius and not a constant, and that malformed path points never crash a builder). Re-rendered both tasks and looked at them: the ring now encloses the grain, the guide starts inside it, and it reads as "this grain". Suite 629. | annotation framing fixed, verified by eye |
| H305 | **A marker has to be bigger than the uncertainty in what it points at.** Three rounds on the same complaint ("the pollen circle is still off tf"), each round ending in a measurement rather than an opinion. Round 1: the anchor came from a path point 46 px down the tube (H304). Round 2: with the ring moved to the path start, the residual to the grain's pixel centroid was still 7.8 px on a 14 px ring radius -- more than half a radius, and the user reported it again. The honest constraint: the grain centre is only knowable to a few px (mean-shift blob snap and a darkness centroid disagreed by ~7 px, and the eye picked a different winner per task), so a 14 px ring on a ~13 px grain can NEVER look reliably centred. Round 3, the fix that holds: measure the blob properly -- threshold, **opening** to sever the contiguous tube (without it the component is 25 x 55 px and the centroid slides down the tube), largest component under the anchor -- and take BOTH position and size from that: centroid (351.3, 387.1) with r_eq 12.5 px for rev8m2-000, (1077.1, 872.1) with r_eq 13.3 px for rev8m2-001, bbox 25x25 and 27x27, i.e. round grains of the size the eye reports; ring radius then 1.5x r_eq floors at 20 px, so the grain sits inside with 6-8 px of margin instead of filling the marker edge to edge. Also: the camera now centres the **ring**, not the ring+guide union (the guide still sets the zoom), which is what "off centre" meant the first time. Verified by rendering each task's own frame with the marker the app will draw and looking at it. Honest residual: the marker is a pointer, not a measurement -- "centre of the dark blob" need not equal "centre of the visually salient patch" on a grain with a dark cap, so a 2-4 px disagreement with a human eye is expected and should not be chased. Suite 633. | annotation marker measured, not guessed |
| H306 | **Every reviewed-extent ever saved was unusable, and the manifest counted them as if they were fine.** Two new masks arrived from the user (274 and 359 stamps, complete, extents recorded), and checking them exposed a bug that had been silently reducing supervision since the extent feature was added: in **every** saved extent across all three projects, the y pair is symmetric about 0 — the signature of napari 0.9.1 reporting a **3-component camera centre (z, y, x)** while the code read `[0], [1]` as (y, x), i.e. z (always 0) became the y centre and the real y became the x centre. The loader then rasterized the extent into the mask's crop, found nothing, and fell back to band-only validity — so a mask flagged `complete` supervised *less* than the flag claims, and `n_masks_with_review_region` reported it as healthy. Fix: read the **last two** components (`ctr[-2], ctr[-1]`), correct for both 2- and 3-component centres, plus two tests (3-component centre maps to the real y; 2-component unchanged). Because the x centre was never recorded, the historical extents are *not* reconstructible and were deliberately left alone: the contract says outside the extent material is unknown, and a guessed extent would supervise pixels the painter never saw. Instead the manifest now reports `n_masks_extent_usable` / `n_masks_extent_unusable` by checking each extent against its own paint (snap20: 8 unusable, 0 usable) so the loss is visible rather than hidden. Two further guards fell out of the same session: the snapshot builder **refuses a project dir passed twice** (snap20 first came out with rev8masks2's two masks duplicated because the rebuild script passed a project its predecessor already listed — the script now unions and dedups), and its "missing db" path was verified to fail closed rather than write an empty snapshot. Suite 638. | annotation contract: honest accounting over silent degradation |
| H307 | **The weight-10 confusable arm collapsed on the full dataset, and the swap metric had been flattering it.** The background A/B finished unevenly -- arm A (weight 0) was reaped at ep11, arm B (weight 10) completed 24 epochs -- and the verdict is a clean negative for the fixed weight: **fullB scores held-out IoU 0.000 at ep11, ep15 and the final checkpoint**, where fullA at ep11 scores 0.217. Silence is the cheap answer when ~83k px of other tubes carry 10x the background weight against ~1-5k px of paint: the negatives outweighed the positive mass 10-20x, so predicting nothing minimised the loss. The matched 2-grain test had measured exactly this term helping (off-diag 0.183 -> 0.062) -- the mechanism transferred, the magnitude did not, which is the small-N trap the ledger warns about, caught here only because the full-scale run was actually run. Worse, the metric had reported "self-peaked=3/3" for those dead checkpoints: `diag >= offdiag` counts a tie as a peak, and `0 >= 0` holds for every column, so **a collapsed model passed the test built to catch query-invariance**. Fixed: a row peaks only STRICTLY and only if the row predicts anything at all, plus a `degenerate` flag that the report prints as `inconclusive(model predicts nothing)`; re-run on fullB/front it now reads 0/3, and the tie case in test_swap_readout now asserts 0/3. Response to the collapse: a scale-free `--confusable-mode balance`, which scales the confusable square so its TOTAL weight never exceeds the paint's (cap = CONFUSABLE_W), so the wrong-instance penalty cannot swamp the tube being learned; contract pinned by test (extra <= n_pos). Launched on snap20 (the two new masks + balance) as `front_snap20`. Honest confound: snap20 differs from snap18 by those two masks, so the balance-vs-fixed comparison mixes two changes; the collapse was categorical, so the "does it stay alive" question is still answered. Suite 643. | criterion 2: fixed weight rejected at full scale, metric error found and fixed |
| H308 | **The body head is a CONSTANT FIELD, so every "0.217 vs 0.183" reading in this ledger is threshold arithmetic, not a measurement of learning.** Found by asking why the balance arm's live-phase checkpoints gave IoU values *identical to the decimal* to the weight-0 baseline. Direct measurement of the head's output on a real mask sample: ep5 logits **min +0.4107 / max +0.4390, std 0.0003** (sigmoid 0.607 everywhere, `frac>=0.5 = 1.000`); ep7 logits all **-0.328**, std 0.0000; ep9 all **-0.364**, std 0.0003. A constant field above 0.5 makes the whole crop "foreground", so IoU = paint / valid-region = 0.217 for the p05 mask and 0.183 for the three clump tubes -- values I had been reading for days as "the model predicts tube-like blobs" and building conclusions on (including H306's swap numbers and H307's A/B verdict). The tell was visible all along in the distance profile (`mean_p=0.500` at every band) and I filtered it out of my greps. Consequences, stated plainly: (1) the fixed-vs-balance confusable comparison has NO verdict -- both arms were constants, and fullB's zeros were a constant below threshold while fullA's 0.217 was a constant above it, two sides of the same non-learning; (2) "query-invariance" is trivially true for a constant field, so it was never evidence about instance discrimination; (3) the single-sample plumbing run reaching whole-mask IoU 1.000 proves the capacity and the loss *can* fit a tube, so the failure is at the full-mixture scale. Next step is the decisive one: overfit three masks at full scale (`can_it_fit`) -- if the head stays constant there, the plumbing is broken at scale; if it learns, the problem is optimisation balance across heads. Suite 643. | the reading that invalidates the previous readings |
| H309 | **The "IoU 1.000" plumbing result was an artifact too: the metric cannot see a flat field.** Acting on H308 I scored the checkpoint that H299 recorded as whole-mask IoU 1.000 (`front_rev8_plumb`) with a crop-wide metric: **crop IoU 0.009**, `pred_frac 1.000`, logit std 0.0056, logit mean +0.524. It is a constant above threshold that marks the entire crop as foreground; 1.000 came from `mask_iou_split`, which scores inside the valid region -- and the valid region of a complete mask is essentially a band around the paint, so "everything is foreground" overlaps the paint almost perfectly there. Same artifact, same session, opposite direction from the 0.000 readings: one constant sits above the threshold, another below, and the metric called one a success and the other a failure. Consequence: **no run in this session has ever localised a tube**, so the swap matrices, distal readouts and confusable A/B of the last days carry no information about whole-instance learning; they were measuring threshold arithmetic. Instrument fixed at the source rather than in my analysis: the trainer's dev probe now prints, for every body-mask dev entry, `body_logit_std`, `body_logit_mean`, `crop_iou`, `pred_frac_crop` and a `flat_field` flag, plus a loud `WARNING ep N: FLAT body field ...` line in the run's own log when it fires -- verified live on a 1-epoch smoke (std 1.4e-05, crop_iou 0.0, warning printed). Criterion 2 therefore restarts from a prior question: can the body head fit ONE tube at all under the current loss? Suite 643. | measurement instrument repaired; criterion-2 work restarted from the right question |
| H310 | **Root cause of the flat body head: the loss, not the model. Plain BCE over a tiny foreground is nearly minimised by a constant field.** Elimination, in order, each step measured rather than argued: the CLIPS are real image data (mean 0.69-0.71, std 0.033-0.039, 100% non-zero, frames verified); the body head's PARAMETERS move between checkpoints (body.weight max|delta| 0.064, bias 0.010); the TRUNK carries rich spatial structure (per-layer spatial std rises 0.017 -> 0.29 through the encoder/decoder, `unet.feat` 0.051); and yet the body output's spatial std is ~0.04 logits (~0.5 +- 0.01 after sigmoid). A bisection over ten checkpoints -- including the shipped `front_v16` -- shows **all of them flat**, so this is not a regression from the recent work: the body head has never localised anything in this session. The mechanism is arithmetic: with ~700 px of paint against 16k-83k px of background, the split-normalised BCE's optimum is still a spatially constant field at p ~= paint/valid, and the head's gradient is dominated by a uniform downward pressure. This is the same disease H298 recorded ("loss preferred a low constant, minimum at p=0.068"); the split-normalisation changed the equilibrium constant but not its flatness. Remedy for tiny-foreground segmentation: an overlap term, which no constant can reduce. Added `soft_dice_loss` + `--body-dice W` (default 0.0 = every previous run's behaviour, so past results stay reproducible), with a unit test pinning that a constant field scores *worse* than a localised one and that all-zero background is not a perfect score either. Testing it now on the single-mask overfit that plain BCE could not fit in 840 updates (`dice_fit`), measured with the honest instrument: crop-wide IoU + logit std. Suite 644. | the loss, not the model: criterion 2 restarts from a fixable cause |
| H311 | **The body head is not broken, it is ~100x too slow, and the fast testbed that shows it is now a script.** After H310's elimination left "the loss" as the suspect, the remaining question was structural vs dynamical. `scripts/head_fit_probe.py` answers it in two minutes: freeze the trunk, optimise ONLY the five parameters of the 1x1 body head against a real mask target (717 px paint, crop (291,5), lr 0.05 -- a strong optimiser on five parameters), and sweep loss shapes. Results after 400 steps: logit spread climbs steadily (split-BCE 0.000 -> 0.046; 20x BCE -> 0.081; Dice+20x BCE -> 0.073; Dice alone is inert, 0.0013) while **crop-IoU stays at 0.009 -- the all-foreground value -- in every configuration**. So the gradient path, the features (std 0.05-0.29 measured layer by layer), the targets (717 px paint in every jittered crop, verified by a debug hook in the trainer) and the parameters (they move) are all fine; the head simply learns two orders of magnitude too slowly to cross threshold within a run's step budget at ~21 updates/epoch. Scaling the loss helps the rate only mildly (20x -> 0.081 logits, still ~30x short of saturation). Next: sweep head LR and un-diluted loss shapes in this harness until crop-IoU actually leaves 0.01, and only then spend hours on a real run -- the mistake this session made repeatedly was launching full runs to answer questions the harness can answer in minutes. Suite 644. | the blocker, isolated to a 2-minute testbed |
| H312 | **The body loss had a degenerate flat valley, and my own H298 fix built it.** Diagnosed in `scripts/head_fit_probe.py` (the 2-minute testbed) and the whole-model variant: with the per-class mass normalisation of H298, the loss's UNIFORM direction has *exactly zero* net gradient -- the positives' pull up and the negatives' pull down cancel identically -- so the loss value sits at **0.6931** (the entropy of a coin flip) while the prediction oscillates between all-foreground and all-background and never learns structure. Measured across loss shapes: split-BCE (incl. x20), Dice+splits, and pos-weighted pixel BCE, every one stayed at crop-IoU 0.009 (= all-foreground) and `pred_frac` ~0.95-1.0; the logit spread grew (0.05 -> 0.65 for lr 0.05 -> 0.5) but never reached the ~3-4 needed to cross threshold. A well-posed objective changes the picture: **soft Dice + a small pixel-normalised BCE** escapes the valley (logit std 0.001 -> **4.4** in 1500 steps, loss 0.66 -> 0.26, predicted area falling 1.0 -> 0.78) and its gradient points at the tube -- but it still needs ~10-20k updates to fit ONE tube, against the ~200-300 updates a full pipeline run provides. So the session's runs were ~50-100x too short *and* optimised a loss with a flat valley: two independent reasons nothing ever localised. Landed: `--body-objective {split,dice_pixel}` (split stays the default so past runs remain reproducible, dice_pixel is the fix), `--body-pixel-bce`, and **per-group learning rates** `--head-lr` (the 1x1 heads need ~150x the trunk's step: measured 0.65 vs 0.046 logit spread for lr 0.5 vs 0.05 over the same 1500 steps). Acceptance test running: 1500 epochs on ONE mask sample with dice_pixel + head lr 0.5, saving every 250 epochs, judged by the honest instrument (crop-IoU must leave 0.01 for the first time in this session). Suite 644. | the loss and the step budget: both were wrong, both now measurable |
| H313 | **My own evaluation script never loaded the weights, so the "flat body head" episode (H308-H311) is void and must be re-derived.** The checkpoints store weights under `model_state`; the evaluator did `model.load_state_dict(sd['model'] if 'model' in sd else sd, strict=False)` -- the dict had no `model` key, so it passed the WHOLE checkpoint dict, `strict=False` matched nothing, and **every "measured" checkpoint was a randomly-initialised model**. That is where "logit std 0.003", "crop-IoU 0.009" and "the head is flat in all ten checkpoints including the shipped v16" came from: a fresh model outputs a constant field whose thresholded area is the whole crop, giving IoU = paint/crop = 0.009. The tell I kept walking past: *identical* numbers across wildly different checkpoints. Fixed in the evaluator (read `model_state` explicitly, warn on missing/unexpected keys). Re-measured with correct loading: `accept_fit/ep749` has logit spread **2.4-2.6** (structure exists), and `replicate_fit/ep99` spread 0.15 after 100 updates. What survives the correction: the trainer's OWN dev-probe readings (it loads the model it trains) genuinely showed flat fields on dev samples, and the trainer's own losses are trustworthy -- the killed jitter run's total loss fell 0.865 -> 0.02, and a fresh 30-epoch trace shows `mean_body_loss` 0.6637 -> 0.6161, i.e. the body term does NOT fit quickly. Also flagged: `--prompt-mix` produced byte-identical loss trajectories for `auto-grain:1.0`, `human:1.0` and `none:1.0`, so either it is inert or the prompt does not reach the body term -- unproven, next thing to check. Lesson for the ledger's own sake: when a measurement says "everything is the same", suspect the instrument before the world. | instrument bug: my conclusions, not the model |
| H314 | **The pipeline DOES learn; my external evaluator is the thing that disagrees, for three separate reasons.** With `--mask-jitter 6` the body loss falls 0.6815 -> 0.0898 in 40 epochs and the front head's error 88 -> 36 px, in ~280 updates. Three configuration facts explain why the same checkpoints measured as "flat"/"mislocalised" through my own script: (1) the evaluator never loaded the weights (H313); (2) with `--mask-jitter 0` the trainer produces ONE update per epoch instead of seven (the epoch line reports `updates_this_epoch: 7.0` with jitter, `1.0` without) -- so my "clean replication" was starved, froze at loss 0.5995 for 1400 epochs, and taught me nothing except that jitter is what generates the sights; (3) the trainer feeds a **temporal window** of 9 frames (`QUERY_OFFSETS = (-8..8)`, query_index 4), while the evaluator fed a single frame -- permanently out of distribution. Fixed (2) and (3) are now documented and the evaluator uses the window; the body loss, front error and dev-row instruments are trustworthy in-distribution. Verified along the way that updates themselves happen: at head-lr 5.0 the body term moves (0.66 -> 0.79, i.e. worse, then plateaus) and at trunk lr 1.0 the loss explodes to 6e11 before recovering, so the gradient path and the optimiser groups work. Acceptance test relaunched through the trainer's own dev rows (`learn3`: three masks, 240 epochs, dev = p05 + dt-005): flat_field / crop_iou / body_logit_std per epoch, which is the one instrument that cannot be fooled by an external mismatch. | the pipeline trains; the method of measuring it was wrong three ways |
| H315 | **Acceptance result: the pipeline can now FIT tubes, and it does not yet GENERALISE (first honest numbers in this ledger for either).** `learn3` (3 train masks, 240 epochs, 3360 updates, dice_pixel objective, head lr 0.5, jitter 6) with dev = the held-out p05 masks: | Instrument notes, all from the trainer's own in-distribution dev rows: **no flat-field warnings anywhere in the run**; body-logit spread on dev entries went 0.008 -> 36-59 (structure exists); the dev IoU values START at 0.398/0.217 and FALL to 0.08-0.15 as the model becomes selective -- the ep-0 numbers were the constant-field artifact (IoU = paint/valid for a fresh model) and the fall is the signature of honest measurement replacing a free lunch. Best honest dev overlap: crop-IoU 0.115 / 0.079 at ep40, settling to ~0.005-0.01. Train-side body loss 0.7315 -> 0.5646 -> 0.2227 -> 0.0673 -> 0.1102 over the run. Conclusion for the rev8 work order: step 2's mechanism (whole-instance learning) is no longer blocked by the loss or the optimiser; what remains is generalisation, which is a data/coverage question (the user's annotation rounds) plus re-running the full mixture on the fixed objective. | H335 | **The WP-B objective, implemented literally, has an all-foreground optimum — found in a 300-step frozen-trunk probe.** `scripts/objective_fit_probe.py` (real paired-clump mask, head only, lr 0.5, 300 steps, both arms from one seed): `dice_pixel` loss 0.8321 -> 0.8128, crop-IoU 0.0394, **pred_frac 0.0107** (sane size, slow fit) vs `dice_selectors` 1.2088 -> **1.0778**, crop-IoU 0.0036, **pred_frac 1.0000** — every-pixel-foreground. Reason: soft Dice masked by the reviewed region counts the reviewed BACKGROUND as positives, so its flat-field gradient is positive everywhere (maximised at p=1); the bg-BCE term at 0.15 cannot outvote dice at 1.0. `rev9_wpB2`'s 30 flat epochs are that pressure, not a learning-rate story. Added `dice_selectors2`: two-class Dice (foreground + background dice averaged) over the same valid region so unknown still contributes exactly zero; optimum is at the paint with correct attachment. Re-probe running. **Also**: `eval_whole_instance.py` and my fit report disagreed on the same checkpoint/crop/queries (0.224 vs 0.007) because each carried its own inference copy — root cause of this whole class of bug. New `prototypes/v30_video_apex/inference.py` owns clip loading, the grain-disc prompt and the threshold; the evaluator reproduces 0.224/0.118/2-of-3/0.363 through it, and the fit-criteria checks (attachment err, spill into foreign, pred frac, all-zero/all-one, pred-frac plausibility) now live in the evaluator's JSON so nothing re-derives a prediction. learn3's checkpoint declares `base: 4` in its config while its weights are multiscale=True, 0 missing / 0 unexpected. |
| H336 | **Correction to H335 — my stated mechanism was wrong, twice.** (1) Masked one-class Dice does NOT prefer painting everything: with saturating and with intermediate logits, the truth is the best field in all three modes (toy crop: truth 0.0013 vs all-one 9.84). (2) The probe's `pred_frac 1.0000` was a **threshold-crossing artifact**, not an all-one optimum: at a near-flat field the mean probability sits within a few 1e-4 of 0.5, so `prob > 0.5` fires on almost every pixel while the field has almost no structure (logit std 0.083). Measured flat-field gradients (paint 9% of the reviewed region, the probe's proportions): at every p0 in {0.1..0.9} the mean gradient ON the paint is NEGATIVE (raise) and OFF the paint POSITIVE (lower) in `dice_selectors`, `dice_selectors2` and `dice_pixel` — the signs are right; the on-paint and off-paint pulls nearly cancel at p=0.5, which is why a flat field drifts slowly either way. What actually separates the objectives is **how fast spatial structure grows**: `dice_pixel` reached logit std 0.161 and crop-IoU 0.039 in 300 frozen-head steps against `dice_selectors`' std 0.083 / IoU 0.0036. The test now pins the property that holds (signs at every flat field, all three modes) instead of the property I imagined; `dice_selectors2` remains available but is NOT established as the fix. |
| H337 | **The decisive set is complete and WP-B is running on it.** The user round-tripped all three `rev8pairs` masks (rev 2 each, `note=ok`, complete, no duplicated stamps — one extent is a 3000-px-wide rectangle from a full zoom-out). `snap24`: **8 body-mask samples, 8 usable, 0 quarantined** (snap20 0/8 → snap21 2/8 → snap22 5/8 → snap24 8/8). `rev9_wpB2` (literal `dice_selectors` on the incomplete snap23) was stopped as superseded; `rev9_wpB4a` started on snap24 with the same five decisive masks, same seed/epochs/base/multiscale, `dice_selectors`. Its first epoch already shows the supervision change: flat-field dice 0.9639 vs wpB2's 0.8503, exactly as larger reviewed regions must affect a masked Dice. Objective probe, all three arms at 300 frozen-head steps: `dice_pixel` logit std **0.161** / IoU 0.0394, `dice_selectors2` **0.118** / 0.0036, `dice_selectors` **0.083** / 0.0036 — structure growth, not the flat-field sign (which is right in all three), is what separates them. | H338 | **Interrupted arms must still be readable.** `history.json` was written only at the end of a run, so a killed or slept-through arm (which is the normal case here) left no trajectory — the exact case the equal-updates comparison needs. `scripts/train_v30_front.py` now rewrites `history.json` every epoch via a temp-file replace; verified live on `rev9_wpB4a` (epoch 0 recorded, 2351 bytes). The code change could not reach the already-running arm (H318 rule), so `rev9_wpB4a` was restarted early rather than left un-instrumented. IoU readings this epoch are threshold artifacts" rather than let a 1.0 pred_frac read as success. Its front/tip heads sat exactly at init (`heat_at_truth -5.06e-13`), expected for a body-only arm. Both rev9 arms now race (sequentially equivalent — threads capped for B): `rev9_wpB4a` = `dice_selectors` (the literal objective) and `rev9_wpB4b` = `dice_pixel` (`--body-dice 1.0 --body-pixel-bce 0.1`), same five decisive masks, snap24, seed 0, base 8, multiscale, 60 epochs, 35 updates/epoch each. `compare_runs.py` now says so plainly when an arm has no history yet instead of raising a bare FileNotFoundError. |

The number that matters is licensed background per mask, computed through the same channel builder the trainer uses: rev8m-000 29,443 px / rev8m-001 22,678 / rev8m-002 7,900 / rev8m2-000 37,499 / rev8m2-001 22,406 / rev8p-000 32,407 / rev8p-001 14,261 / rev8p-002 40,815, against paints of 298-1,076 px — every mask now carries a substantial verified background (10-100x its paint), which is the review's supervision condition. Two apparent gaps in the rendered sheet (no cyan box visible for rev8p-001, no blue shading for rev8p-002) are display artifacts of a paint-centred crop, not data gaps: both licence 14,261 and 40,815 px respectively.
