| H341 | **Arm A (literal `dice_selectors`, complete supervision, 60 epochs) predicts nothing.** Final checkpoint through the shared inference path on the pinned clump crop: `diag 0.000 vs off-diag 0.000, 0/3 rows peak, spread 0.000`, every per-query `pred_frac 0.0`, `all_zero True`. Training loss was flat (1.6994 -> 1.6923 over 60 epochs / 2100 updates). Not the objective's optimum: at the total level the selectors are balanced (dice <=1, self <=0.5, bg <=0.15, foreign capped at the paint mass), so an all-background field costs ~1.5 while the truth costs ~0.001 — the run is stuck far from a good basin, it did not find a bad optimum. The consistent explanation is DILUTION: the same objective and head-lr escaped the initial all-background basin under the old ~2k-px reviewed regions but not under the new ~30k-px ones, i.e. per-pixel gradients fell 10-100x exactly as the reviewed area grew (H311's arithmetic, now on the supervision side). Next experiment, at equal updates: Dice normalized against the PAINT mass instead of the whole reviewed region, keeping the separate reviewed/foreign BCE terms (their job is punishing wrong tubes, and the foreign term is already capped). |
| H342 | **Equal-updates verdict: at this supervision scale the objective choice does not matter — both arms fail identically.** `rev9_wpB4a` (literal `dice_selectors`) and `rev9_wpB4b` (`dice_pixel`, `--body-dice 1.0 --body-pixel-bce 0.1`), same five decisive masks, snap24, seed 0, base 8, multiscale, 60 epochs, 35 updates/epoch each (2,100 updates apiece), manifests identical except the objective: both flat, both final checkpoints all-background on the pinned clump (`diag 0.000, 0/3 peaks, pred_frac 0.0, all_zero True`). Since the same objectives *did* escape under the old tighter reviewed regions, the scale of the supervision — not the objective — is the blocker. Fix implemented and probing: a class-balanced Dice term (`set_body_dice_balanced`, `--body-dice-balanced`) weighting each class by its own mass (paint 1/(2 n_pos), background 1/(2 n_neg), unknown exactly 0), so the paint's per-pixel pull rises ~50x; the truth is its optimum (loss 0), all-one 0.33, all-zero 1.0. Probe runs the same 300 frozen-head steps with it before any hour-long arm is spent on it. |
| H343 | **Movie workflow runs end to end on current code (WP-D plumbing verified, quality unproven).** `scripts/run_v30_movie.py` on one real event (`obs-r4-p05`, frame 49350, snap24) with the 60-epoch arm and the tip-cnn weights: checkpoint rebuilt by the model factory from its own declaration (`{'variant': 'temporal', 'base': 8, 'multiscale': True}`, groups head=0.5/trunk=0.003), 46 proposals scored to 40 candidates, selection written, and all four artifacts produced (candidates/measurements/report/selection.json). Its own report says `n_opportunities 1, oracle 1.0, selected 1.0, median_selected_error_px 3.37` inside a 5-px tolerance, with `n_negative_gold 0` — and it also prints `false_emissions_on_negatives: 0` beside that zero negative count, which is exactly the reading the review warns not to take as success. n=1 is not a quality claim; the route blocker (H295: 0/8 automatic selection, median 73 px error on the 8-event set) is unchanged. ALSO: the first arm-C launch died on `NameError: set_body_dice_balanced` (my import patch had not landed; `py_compile` cannot catch it). Fixed, then a 1-epoch pre-flight to a scratch dir (exit 0, checkpoint written) before the real 60-epoch launch — the rule now: py_compile is not a smoke test. |
| H344 | **The band channel had never been populated in the raster path — the real reason every arm was flat.** `body_mask_channels_from_raster` computed the paint-adjacent band ONLY inside the `not used_extent` branch, i.e. the band was the quarantine FALLBACK. So the moment every extent became usable (snap24: 8/8), the channel was zeros on all 8 masks while `bg_reviewed` held 7,900-82,559 px — and the new `--body-bg-region band` knob therefore had nothing to select, which is how it was caught (the pre-flight's background and foreign terms contributed exactly 0.0: loss = dice 0.344 + self 0.0226). Fixed to compute the band unconditionally (this module's own docstring always described it as a named channel, `valid = fg | band | bg_reviewed`). Band masses are now 1,587-3,162 px per mask — the scale the trainer actually moved at under the old supervision. One test encoded the old behaviour (`assert ch["band"].sum() == 0, "reviewed extent replaces the band"`); it now asserts the documented semantics (band > 300 px, disjoint from fg, every band pixel within 13.5 px of the paint) via a distance transform. Suite 711 passed + that one revised. Pre-flight with band negatives: dev `flat_field: False`, `body_logit_std 1.93` at epoch 0 — the first non-flat body field in the entire rev9 series (all earlier arms: std ~0, flat True, every epoch). `rev9_wpB6` launched, one change from arm C. |
| H345 | **Arm D (band negatives) is in, and the honest reading is: still flat, but for a newly-isolated reason.** `rev9_wpB6` 60 epochs: training body 0.7484 -> 0.7349 (the learn3-era scale, vs 1.69/1.21 for arms A/B/C), but the dev row is BIT-IDENTICAL across all 60 epochs (std 0.0, mask_iou 0.0787, prox 0.1442, dist 0.0541) and the clump matrix is `diag 0.015, 1-of-3 degenerate, spread 0.000`. Checked directly rather than assuming the probe was broken: weights DO move (44 of 56 tensors differ ep19 vs ep59, body.weight by 0.143), the dev clip is a real crop (0.12-0.85, native 1280x1024, mask bbox 845,103 24x78), and the final checkpoint's body output on that crop is a CONSTANT +1.588 (std 1.2e-07, frac>0.5 = 1.0). So: training happens, the body head still produces a constant on held-out material, and the dev probe is telling the truth. Also found: my two new knobs were not recorded in the checkpoint config (`body_dice_balanced: None`, `body_bg_region: None`) — the same omission class WP-A.4 fixed for ten others — now recorded along with `trunk_lr`. Next lever, with evidence: the trunk moved only ~0.015 in 60 epochs at trunk lr 0.003 while the head moved 0.14 at 0.5, i.e. the trunk may simply be too slow on this supervision. Test at 10 epochs (13 min) before committing an hour: trunk lr 0.003 vs 0.01 vs 0.03, same everything else. |
| H346 | **The learning-rate ladder says the optimizer is not the blocker; the supervision scale is.** 10 epochs each on the same five masks: trunk lr 0.01 -> body 0.7349, dev std 1.2e-07 (still constant); trunk lr 0.03 -> body 0.7349 but dev std 2.5e+07, i.e. divergent logits. Neither produces structure. Diagnostic that follows: learn3 trained under QUARANTINED extents, so its `valid` was paint+band (~2-3k px) and its Dice was never diluted by tens of thousands of reviewed-background pixels; every rev9 arm trains under the corrected 40k-px extents and stays constant. Added `--body-dice-region {extent,band}`: with "band" the Dice is evaluated over paint+13.5px band (learn3's scale) while the reviewed extent still defines what is VERIFIED and evaluation still measures over it in full — focusing the loss is not shrinking the claim. Also fixed: my config patch had referenced `a.trunk_lr`, a flag that does not exist (the trunk rate is `--lr`); every future run would have died at startup. Pre-flight clean and the consumption table now declares `body_band: 5`. `rev9_wpB7` launched (band dice region + band bg region + balanced dice), 60 epochs, reading the epoch-10 dev row for the verdict. |
| H347 | **ROOT CAUSE FOUND: a dead ReLU on the temporal Conv3d blocked every head — and the fix unblocks learning.** Measured with a stage-by-stage diagnostic on a real crop: the encoder's feature maps carry spatial std 0.124, the temporal Conv3d's output is then constant, `relu` zeroes it, and the body head can only return its bias. Direct proof on `rev9_wpB7`'s checkpoint: the temporal pre-activation is uniformly NEGATIVE (min -0.690, max -0.042), so `relu` -> exactly 0 for every input; `body` then emits a constant (std 0.0, mean 1.3663, bit-identical across three different masks/prompts/clips). Gradient through a dead ReLU is exactly zero, so the layer cannot recover, the heads see no spatial signal, and NO objective, learning rate or supervision region could ever have changed the outcome. This single defect explains all six flat arms, the bit-identical dev rows, and the rev9 audit's image-independence finding (0.984/0.983 IoU). Secondary measurement: the encoder's features carry only 3% spatial vs 97% channel-offset variation (0.004 vs 0.133) — the collapse reached into the trunk. Fix: `leaky_relu(negative_slope=0.01)` on the temporal layer, so it cannot die. The fix cannot revive existing checkpoints (their weights encode the collapsed solution), so it was tested on a fresh 5-epoch run: dev std 0.361 / 0.266 / 1.229 / 1.646 at eps 0-3, `flat False` at every epoch, and the body loss actually falling (1.025 -> 0.751 by ep3) — the first real learning in the rev9 series. `rev9_wpB7` was stopped mid-flight (it trained through the dead ReLU for 33 epochs; its result was already understood). `rev9_wpB8` launched: same settings, 60 epochs, with the fix. |
| H348 | **First live body model: tube-shaped predictions with a plausible extent, ownership not yet.** The 5-epoch run through the leaky-relu fix (H347) ends at dev mask_iou **0.575** (from 0.080 at ep0 — the first held-out structure in the rev9 series) and its three-query matrix on the pinned clump reads: g1's query on its own tube **0.372** (proximal 0.65, distal 0.27), the others 0.06-0.07; `pred_frac 0.070` of the crop — `pred_frac_plausible: True` for the FIRST time (every earlier arm either predicted ~30% of the crop or nothing at all); distal IoUs 0.23-0.44, so it traces tube BODIES, not just the tip end. Ownership is the remaining gap, and it is concrete: 1/3 rows peak on their own tube, diag 0.167 vs off-diag 0.175, and the g0 query fires on g1's tube (0.46) far better than on its own (0.067) — cross-talk between neighbours, which is exactly the question WP-B/WP-C exists to answer, now measurable rather than moot. Attachment error 11.2-11.4 px, spill into the other tubes 0.12-0.15. Evidence: `runs/prototypes/v30/framing_checks/threequery_relucheck.png` (+ threequery_relucheck.json). `rev9_wpB8` (60 epochs, same settings) is running and will show whether ownership emerges with training. |
| H349 | **rev9_wpB8 (60 epochs, dead-ReLU fix): the body GEOMETRY works; ownership does not.** Training: body loss 1.025 -> 0.285 (ep42) with `flat False` and dev std 0.7-3.4 at EVERY epoch — the first honest learning curve in the rev9 series. Dev traj oscillates (iou 0.59 ep18, 0.55 ep48, 0.32 ep24, 0.08 ep30/54/59), so the FINAL checkpoint is one of the bad points (matrix diag 0.015) while the best saved one is not — evaluated `ep49.pt`: dev iou 0.549, proximal 0.571, **distal 0.530** (the review's "preserved distal body" criterion met on held-out material), and on the pinned clump every query returns a TUBE-SHAPED answer with IoU 0.54-0.60 (prox 0.84, distal 0.44 on its best tube). The failure is now specific and isolated: all three queries answer with the SAME tube (g1's). diag 0.254 vs off-diag 0.252, 1/3 rows peak, row spread 0.064 — the owner conditioning does not discriminate. So the body head can segment tubes; the query/owner path is what fails to distinguish neighbouring instances — exactly the owner-conditioning question the review's WP-B names, and the reviewer's proposed remedy (owner-independent image encoder with cached frame features and an owner-conditioned decoder) is the design to build next. Note on selection honesty: ep49 is chosen by dev IoU, which is a selection; the last checkpoint's numbers are reported alongside it. Also: `--save-every 5` paid for itself (ep4..ep59 saved) — the earlier reap at ep17 cost everything. |
| H350 | **Consultant handoff written**: `prototypes/HANDOFF_2026-09-16_rev9.md` (supersedes `HANDOFF.md` of the same morning; the older files are left untouched — the H316 clobber lesson). Contents: the one-screen state, the dead-ReLU root cause with its measurement chain, the full honest run table (including the four pre-fix failures, the lr ladder, and the post-fix runs), the three-query ownership table on the pinned clump, a work-package-by-work-package account (A.1-A.6 done; B measured with two of my own claims corrected; C first stage done and the fine-tune stage blocked on the GPU decision; D integrated), what the reviewer's list still needs (owner conditioning first, then training stability, the owned-absence label, the >=0.90 fit target), the traps that cost time here, verify-first commands, and where everything lives. Suite at the time of writing: 712 passed / 0 failed (one test's forward-replay helper had to be re-aligned with model.py after the leaky-relu fix — it caught the change, which is what it exists for). |
| H351 | **rev10 audit reproduced in full, then four objective defects repaired with exit evidence.** Reproduced in `runs/prototypes/v30/rev10-audit-repro/` (their scripts, scratch copy): own-only and own+union losses identical to full precision on clump views (0.0012975569115951657 both); `body_dice_balanced` computed `_w` and never used it (0.846 -> 0.9998 as background grows; documented formula 0.4999995); three masks with paint outside their extent (97/979, 310/810, 345/548) and bands outside it too (626/1581/1373 px); `allow_route_head_gap=True` accepting a checkpoint missing `body.weight`. Repairs, all in `train.py` unless noted: (1) the band now restricts the ORDINARY-BACKGROUND term only — the foreign-exclusive selector `F_i` is never gated by it; (2) band negatives are intersected with licensed review scope (`body_bg_reviewed`, newly plumbed from the builder by `train_v30_front.py`); (3) the balanced Dice actually applies `_w`; (4) a zero-paint owned-absence sample keeps a rejection term via `ABSENCE_PAINT_FLOOR` (500 px) instead of a cap proportional to zero paint; (5) ambiguous multi-owner overlap is excluded from `F_i` (`paint_overlap_union`, new in `targets.py`, plumbed as `body_overlap`); (6) the selector branch honours `body_dice_weight()`, which it had silently ignored (a `--body-dice 0.0` probe still carried a 0.9407 dice term), and the trainer now declares the selector-mode default 1.0 explicitly; (7) a `no-owner-prompt` update carries ZERO owner-specific loss (the 418-update defect), with the count in `body_sources`; (8) `tests/test_rev10_contracts.py` pins all of it through the COMPLETE configured loss. Measured outcome (`scripts/rev10_objective_probe.py --jitter`, 40 views = 8 masks x 5 offsets): **15/15 views with licensed foreign pixels separate own-only from own+union**, min foreign gradient **+2.87e-4** with the farthest at 87 px from the band, `all_foreign_grads_positive: True`; 5 views carry foreign pixels OUTSIDE the reviewed scope and report them separately as correctly-zero. A 1-epoch smoke run confirms it reaches real runs: `mean_body_foreign_bce 0.3124` live, `body_sources {'own-mask-raster': 31, 'no-owner-prompt': 4}`. Suite 712 -> 718 passed / 0 failed. |
| H352 | **rev10 WP-A continued: checkpoint exceptions restricted to an exact key allowlist, plus semantic versioning.** The factory's `allow_route_head_gap` used to strip route-head keys and re-load with `strict=False`, so a checkpoint missing `body.weight` loaded silently (audit fixture, reproduced). It now loads permissively ONCE, judges the report against `LEGACY_WEIGHT_GAP = {route_head.weight, route_head.bias}` exactly, and refuses any other missing/unexpected key or shape failure — the permitted gap is still REPORTED, never swallowed. New semantic contract: `CURRENT_MODEL_SCHEMA = 2`, `CURRENT_ACTIVATION = "leaky_relu:0.01"`, `CURRENT_PREPROCESSING` (native/255 grayscale, 9-frame QUERY_OFFSETS window, native-pixel crop origin); `save_checkpoint` writes all three; the factory refuses a checkpoint that declares no semantics unless the caller explicitly asks for a declared replay (`allow_legacy_semantics`), and refuses one that declares a DIFFERENT activation even then (those weights must be replayed under their own architecture). Wired into trainer/evaluator/movie runner. `tests/test_model_factory.py` gains three contract tests (undeclared-semantics refusal + declared replay; wrong declared activation refused even with the replay flag; the legacy gap covers route-head keys only and is reported). Suite 721. |
| H353 | **rev10 WP-A: accounting after actual loss selection, and no swallowed consumers.** The trainer counted labels at target-BUILD time, which over-counts: the audited ledger's 12 body keys were five independent masks plus bookkeeping, and three "consumed" confusable labels had zero real contribution. It now also counts what the loss actually consumed — a per-mask counter incremented only when the sample's target has mass AFTER the no-prompt rule — written to `label_usage.json` as `actual_contributions` alongside the build-time notes, so the difference is visible. The consumption table's off-map check now RAISES (it used to record `off_consumer_map` and continue: "records an off-map band consumer without failing"), with `strict=False` kept for audit tooling, and the new channels (`body_band`, `body_bg_reviewed`, `body_overlap`) are on-map for `body_mask`. The trainer's `except (TypeError, ValueError): # never fail a run on bookkeeping` is narrowed to TypeError only, so contract failures stop a run. Per-selector logging added: the loss publishes `body_mass_self`/`body_mass_bg`/`body_mass_foreign` (to `out`, never `_parts`, which is summed into the loss) and the trainer records head/trunk gradient L2 magnitudes from the step that just ran. First attempt placed the mass export before `_n_self` existed (`UnboundLocalError`, 15 tests red) — caught by the suite, moved after the definition. Suite 722 passed / 0 failed. |
| H354 | **rev10 WP-B first fit check (60 steps, all three owners in one step): the corrected objective learns, the fit gate is not met.** `scripts/rev10_fit_check.py` on the pinned clump (snap24, frame 42000, crop 475,518) from `rev9_wpB8/ep29.pt`, three owners' prompts and targets in a single optimization step, instrumented every block. Result: loss 8.22 -> 1.32; foreign supervision live in every block (2,462 px, never the zero-contribution case the review forbids); head grads 0.09-2.4, trunk grads 6.6-30 (both groups learning); body fields non-flat (std 0.08-0.17); g1 IoU 0.080 -> 0.401 and recall 0.161 -> 0.779; g2 recall -> 0.961; and the decisive signature that was structurally impossible before the repair — **rival positive rate falling 0.56 -> 0.20** as the foreign term bites. NOT passed: IoU 0.13/0.40/0.19 (gate ~0.90), rivals 0.20 (gate <0.05), one owner's recall below 0.95. 60 steps is too short to conclude the architecture is the limit, so a 300-step run with the same instrumentation, abort rules and per-block native prediction saves (npz + PNG) is in flight; the 60-step result and its predictions are preserved as the pre-registered baseline. This is a fit test on a developed scene, not generalization. |
| H355 | **rev10 WP-A: separate best-body / best-front checkpoints, hash-bound.** The run manifest selected one "best" on a FRONT metric even while the front head was untrained (the review). The trainer now keeps each head's own best: `best_body_epNN.pt` on the dev body-mask reviewed-region mask_iou (mean over dev body rows) and `best_front_epNN.pt` on the mean dev front_err_px, each with a `best_<kind>.json` pointer naming the metric DOMAIN, the value, the epoch and a sha256 parameter hash of the saved weights. Immutable checkpoints, so a tie never overwrites. |
| H356 | **rev10 WP-C: the candidate contract fields the review found empty are now exported.** The fresh 290-candidate export carried zero `current_path` and zero `front_s` fields ("Use one candidate contract everywhere"). The runner now records, per candidate: `support_path` (the proposed route), `front_s` and `support_s` (the front head's arclength cutoff and the support end), `current_path` (the measured portion, previously computed and discarded), `current_tip`, `coord_transform` (native crop origin + "no resampling"), `physical_origin` (grain centre marked identity-only with length starting at the proximal path end — the review forbids adding a grain-centre-to-exit distance to biological length, and these labels do not yet carry a grain-boundary exit), `visibility`/`status`, plus the A.5 scorer fields. Verified by a run: 40 candidates, `front_s 111.99`, `current_tip` distinct from the support end, all fields present. `candidates.normalize_candidate` copies rows wholesale, so v2 schema normalization carries them unchanged. |
| H357 | **rev10 WP-A: device parity measured — and the canary caught the defect the review predicted.** `scripts/rev10_device_canary.py` runs a forward+backward at 288x288x9 on every available device and compares against CPU. First run reproduced `RuntimeError: Passed CPU tensor to MPS op`: `_owner_channel` and `sample_ribbon` constructed every tensor on CPU regardless of the model's device (7 constructions fixed to follow `frames.device`). Second finding was MY canary's bug — it built a fresh, differently-seeded model per device and reported a 0.404 "parity difference"; with an identical seed the two agree to 1.12e-08. Result on this machine: CPU 2.01 s/step, **MPS 0.74 s/step (2.7x faster) with parity 1.12e-08**, `parity_ok: True` — so training here can use MPS, and any future CUDA run enters through the same gate rather than a hope. Inference helpers (`predict_queries`) also now follow the model's device instead of hardcoding CPU. Suite 722 passed / 0 failed. |
| H358 | **rev10 WP-C: metric domains made explicit, report separated, acquisition time declared.** (1) `eval_route_evidence.py` scored the full support polyline (the review: "the route evaluator still measures full support polylines"); it now scores the candidate's `current_path` — the portion truncated at the model's front arclength cutoff — with the fallback recorded as `measured_field` on every row (verified: `measured_field: "current_path"` for every rule on a live run). (2) `report.json` gains `proposal_coverage`, `selection_accuracy` and `final_measurement_error` as separate blocks, plus a `measurement_domain` note: path field, the length origin (proximal end of the proposed support path; grain centre is identity only and does NOT enter the length), the time semantics and "native pixels, no resampling". (3) Every candidate now carries `source_frame` AND `source_time_s` with a DECLARED `--acquisition-cadence-s`; the manifest records no cadence and the runner does not invent one — undeclared stays null, because playback FPS does not establish biological growth rate. Verified live: frame 49350 -> source_time_s 148050.0 at the declared 3.0 s cadence. |
| H359 | **rev10 WP-C: pred.body is now consumed, exported and ablatable.** The review: "The movie runner never uses `pred.body`" (verified: no reference anywhere in the runner) and "Run a body ablation: removing or swapping its probabilities must change routes". Every candidate now records `body_support` sampled along its OWN route (`mean`/`max`/`frac_above_half`) plus the ablation mode, and a declared `score_body = sel x body_support_mean`; `--body-ablation {none,zero,swap}` removes the spatial signal or replaces it with a constant. Verified live on one event: `none` gives body_mean 0.1442 / frac 0.1438, `zero` gives 0.0/0.0, and both arms keep the same default winner — correct, because the default ranking still sorts by `sel` and the review forbids optimizing selection before the fit gate. The ablation mechanism and the exposed inputs are therefore proven; using them in the selection policy is the deliberate next step once the gate result is in. |
| H360 | **Front/route heads at initial weights: root cause is the RUN SCOPE, not missing data.** The rev10 review found the current model's front convolution, front head and route head still at their initial weights and read it as a data/training gap. Verified facts: (1) snap24's `path_tip` samples carry 10/10 two-or-more-point `path_xy` (the FULL spans ARE in the snapshot; `lanes_a`/`lanes_b` are genuinely empty, and the oracle route is built inside the trainer by `blind_oracle_route(path_xy, tip_xy)`, not stored); (2) `rev9_wpB8` ran with `--only-refs` = the five decisive body masks — `n_train_samples: 5` in its own recorded config — so it never saw a path, tube or tip sample at all. The heads are at init because the run was body-scoped, not because the supervision was lost. Two of my own intermediate queries during this diagnosis were wrong (I searched `path_native`, which does not exist — the field is `path_xy` — and concluded a regression that was not there); the corrected measurement is the one above. |
| H361 | **rev10: three defects found by the 1-epoch pre-flight before a long run could reach them.** (1) `movie_aliases` collapse in `samples_from_snapshot` rewrote `sample.movie` but not the movie prefix inside `entry_id`, so `assert_movie_qualified` refused `m2|obs-r3-003|102530` for a sample whose movie was already `m1` — the collapse now rewrites the prefix; (2) `_group_grad_norms()` was referenced from the epoch loop but never defined after the gradient-logging patch (a `NameError` on the first real step — wpB8 predated the patch, so nothing had executed the path); it is now defined next to the single `_head_prefixes` definition that both the optimiser groups and the logging use; (3) `sample_tensors` read `_bt` unconditionally while assigning it only in the body-mask branch, so EVERY ribbon-path sample raised `UnboundLocalError` — the full-sample run could never have started. All three are exactly the failure mode the pre-flight exists for: `py_compile` passes, the code looks right, and a multi-hour run dies. |
| H362 | **The dev split must include the dev ref's own frame.** The full-sample front run refused to start: the split guard found 1 (movie, frame) in both train and dev — `('ld', 49350)`, the frame of the held-out ref `r4-p05` — because with 135 samples (not 5) other supervision exists at that frame. Fixed by holding out `ld:11831,ld:49350`. The guard behaved exactly as designed; the fix is on my side. |
| H363 | **rev10 WP-B fit verdict: the repaired objective FITS PARTIALLY — the pass bar is not met, so the review's architecture change is next.** 300 steps (35 updates/epoch, five decisive views, per-block writes): loss 8.220 -> 0.405 (20x), flat at 0.41 over the last ~120 steps (a plateau, not a crash — gradients alive throughout: hg 3e-02, tg 6.6e+00 at the last step). Per owner at step 299: g1 IoU 0.800 / recall 0.832 / **rival 0.034 (INSIDE the <0.05 gate)**; g0 IoU 0.242 / recall 0.993 / rival 0.142 (its best was **0.018 at step 209**); g2 IoU 0.269 / recall 1.000 / rival 0.131 (best 0.060). Recall is essentially solved (0.83-1.00) and the rivals improved ~4x from 0.56, but 2 of 3 owners sit at 0.13-0.14 against a <0.05 gate and IoU is 0.24-0.27 against ~0.90. Compare step 60: 0.132/0.401/0.190 — the run kept improving well past 60, then flattened. Verdict: the objective CAN fit on one owner and demonstrates real structure on all three, but does not clear the pass bar; the review's decision rule ("if it cannot fit, make the architecture change") applies, and the late-block oscillation of g0/g2 rivals is consistent with the 5-view batch rather than divergence (the loss itself is stable). |
| H364 | **`path_tip -> body_band/body_bg_reviewed/body_overlap` is sanctioned in CONSUMER_MAP, with the reason stated.** The off-map rule (H352) correctly refused the scoped front run: a path sample with a LINKED body mask really is body-supervised, so it emits the mask's own region channels. A ribbon-only path (no mask) emits none of them — the band is built inside the linked-mask branch — so the sanction cannot launder an unsupervised region into the loss. 11 ledger/contract tests pass. |
| H365 | **The scoped front run is the right scope, and the pre-flight found four more things before it.** Full-set (135 samples) epochs take ~50 min, which makes 60 epochs pointless; `--only-refs "...mask refs...,r4-p"` gives 12 training samples (the 10 path_tip FULL spans + linked masks) at ~5 min/epoch. Pre-flight fixes along the way: (1) `pkill -f "train_v30_front.py"` killed the launcher shell itself (its own command line matches) — never pattern-kill by a string the launching command contains; (2) the off-map refusal above; (3) confirmed the alias-prefix, `_group_grad_norms` and `_bt` fixes from H361 hold on a real run; (4) first live exercise of the best-body/best-front checkpoints (H355): `best_body_ep0.pt`, `best_front_ep0.pt`, their `.resolved.json` and pointers all written. The run then started for real: epoch 0 loss 5.9068 with `mean_dev_front_px 24.0` and live `route: 5`/`apex_pos: 5` label consumption — the front/route heads are receiving supervision for the first time. |
| H366 | **rev10 WP-B: the architecture change is built, tested and running its fit arm.** Per the review's exact wording (line 125): image-only encoder whose features are cacheable per frame/clip (`ImageOnlyEncoder`), prompts supplied to a SEPARATE owner-conditioned decoder (`OwnerConditionedDecoderNet`, `variant="owner-decoder"`), built on the temporal interface so the fit check, evaluator and runner take it behind one flag; and the review's "residual query-frame feature path into the body decoder" is explicit — the body head reads `cat([mean-fused volume, query-frame volume slice])` so mean-fusion cannot erase spatial evidence. The review's constraint is honoured structurally: ownership enters ONLY as spatial mask prompts, never as an id embedding, and a test asserts that swapping the owner STRING with identical geometry changes nothing. Encoder equivalence to `_MultiScaleUNet`'s encoder is asserted layer-for-layer under identical weights (verified True) so the cache can never silently disagree with the model it mirrors. Three tests pass: mirror equivalence, no owner-id embedding, query-frame residual reaches the body head. Fit arm running: `--variant owner-decoder --steps 60` (block 30), then the equal-updates comparison against a from-scratch temporal arm — the review's "compare equal samples/updates". |
| H367 | **The front head is training for the first time, and the numbers move.** With the run scope corrected (H360-H365), `rev10_front20` epoch 0 -> 8: train loss 5.91 -> 4.38, body loss 1.17 -> 0.53, and the front error (the dev probe AND the train-side `mean_front_err_px`, two independent readings) falls 24.0 -> 18.99 -> 15.98 -> 15.49 px. This is the first evidence in the project that the front head is not frozen at initialization: the rev10 review's finding that "its front convolution, front head and route head are still at their initial weights" is answered by running them against the supervision that was always present but never sampled. |
| H368 | **rev10 WP-B: the owner-decoder arm's fit probe found two real bugs before the run, and its first 60-step scratch baseline is the reason the transfer arm exists.** (1) `--init-encoder-from` first transferred 16 of 22 tensors: the od model stores the encoder as `enc.enc.N.*`, so `"unet." + name` missed it while *silently copying the temporal model's DECODER, feat and heat* (same shapes). Copying that decoder is wrong twice over — its weights expect features produced WITH the owner channel, and training the copy at head rate diverged (loss 7478, head grad 1.4e+05). Transfer is now encoder-only, name-mapped through the mirror, first conv trimmed from image+owner to image-only: 22/22 tensors, equivalence re-verified True. (2) The 60-step scratch baseline (decoder at trunk lr 0.003 — the same rate the temporal arm used on its PRETRAINED decoder) reached g1 IoU 0.383 with rivals 0.943: it paints everything, because at 0.003 the only thing that can move is the 1x1 body head. So the fresh-decoder arm is declared at head rate 0.05 with the encoder at 0.003, and the grouping is printed by the script rather than implied. The 300-step arm is running. |
| H369 | **The front head's first trained trajectory, and the best-checkpoint machinery justified on its first real use.** `rev10_front20` (20 epochs, 12 samples, 340 updates): dev front error 24.0 -> 18.99 -> 15.98 -> **3.95 px at ep16**, train-side 24.1 -> **6.63 px at ep15** — then a late spike (dev 82.3/87.3 at ep17-18, train 87.7 at ep19). The best-front tracker (H355) saved ep3/8/14/**16**, so the good model survived the spike with its metric domain ("mean dev front_err_px") and param hash recorded; `best_body_ep0` kept 0.495 dev mask IoU, the run's best. This is the first time in the project that a learned front localizes a cap at single-digit pixels, and the first time the separate best-body/best-front checkpoints did real work. |
| H370 | **rev10 WP-C: the interval run separates availability from selection, and the report stops lying in three ways.** `rev10_interval3` (6 events, best_front_ep16 driving): proposal availability is proven — the gold tip lies 0.011-1.46 px from SOME PROPOSED candidate's polyline in 6/6 events (verified by reading the diagnostic loop: it iterates `candidates`, not the oracle-substituted pool, so this is the proposer's own coverage). Selection is the bottleneck — the winner is the closest candidate in 0/6 events, with the selected endpoint 5.1-117 px from the gold tip. Three report defects found while reading it: (1) `route_source` was absent from every exported candidate (all 220 read None), so automatic and oracle could not be separated — `export_candidates` now defaults it to "proposed" and the count of oracle substitutions is reported per event; (2) the `proposal_coverage` block I added earlier reported `oracle_route_available` from a snapshot flag that is False every run — it now DERIVES `front_available` from the closest proposed candidate against a named 15 px tolerance; (3) the summary's `coverage=0.00 oracle=0.00` over an EMPTY gold set looked like a failed measurement, so the line now prints `n_gold` and marks the vacuous case explicitly. Verified on a fresh 2-event run: provenance {'proposed': 78}, `front_available: true` at 0.096/0.982 px, honest zeros. |
| H371 | **rev10 WP-B: the owner-decoder arm's optimization pathology, measured in three probes.** (1) The 300-step arm at head lr 0.05 ABORTED ITSELF at step 30 — the fit check's own guard: "dead body branch (flat field)", body std exactly 0.000 and trunk grad exactly 0.00e+00. (2) Not the H347 family: with leaky activations in the fresh decoder (encoder deliberately left as the exact ReLU mirror so the transfer stays valid) the field STILL collapsed — std 0.056 -> 0.004 -> 0.002 by step 39 with gradients alive (hg 0.046, tg 0.037) and the loss barely moving (4.335 -> 4.316). So it was not a dead branch but an AdamW step-size pathology on a freshly initialised decoder: at 1r 0.05 the fastest loss reduction available to a net whose features are still random is to saturate the field to a constant. (3) At head lr 0.01 the same arm stays alive and LEARNS: std 0.003 -> 0.158, loss 4.316 -> 2.230 in 40 steps, g1 IoU 0.325 and rising, recall 0.94. The declared setting for the architecture comparison is therefore head lr 0.01 (decoder+heads) with the transferred encoder at 0.003; the 300-step equal-updates arm is running. Every number here comes from the script's own per-block instrumentation. |
| H372 | **rev10 WP-B VERDICT: the owner-decoder architecture does NOT beat the corrected current architecture at equal updates — the architecture change is not justified by the fit test.** Equal samples/updates (300 steps, same script, same repaired objective, same 5-view clump), final block: | arm | loss | g0 IoU/rival | g1 IoU/rival | g2 IoU/rival | | temporal (H363) | **0.405** | 0.242/0.142 | 0.800/**0.034** | 0.269/0.131 | | owner-decoder (encoder transferred, decoder+heads fresh, declared head lr 0.01) | 1.200 | 0.159/0.469 | 0.801/0.571 | 0.176/0.571 |. Recall is high in both (0.89-1.00) and g1's IoU ties (0.801 vs 0.800), but the temporal arm fits the objective ~3x better and — decisive for a change motivated by OWNERSHIP — separates owners far better (rivals 0.03-0.14 against 0.25-0.59). The od arm was still descending at step 299 (2.57 -> 1.20 over steps 59-299 while the temporal arm plateaued from ~step 119), so more steps might close the loss gap, but the ownership gap is the metric the change was for. Caveats stated honestly: the od arm trains a FRESH decoder against a fully pretrained temporal arm, and only the lr where it learns at all (0.01; 0.05 collapses to a flat field) was used — I am not tuning it against the fit gate. Conclusion: with supervision repaired (six flat arms -> real structure on all three owners, one through the gate), the architecture change does not move the fit; the next lever per the review's own order is supervision QUANTITY and coverage — the annotation round that is open now. |
| H373 | **rev10 round-3 close-out: what the round banked, and the absent-case lesson.** Banked: three EMERGENCE-STUB body masks (frames 26460/27720/28770, 39/43/72 stamps) — all three lie 100% within 10 px of the same tube trajectory (0.4-0.7 px from the v29 path once it starts at 28980), and the v29 trace misses the entire earliest-growth regime (it has no path before 28980). The OWNED-ABSENCE case failed four times, always the same way: my "absence" frames each had a real stub that the user could see and paint (he was right every time; my trace/vision reads were wrong every time), and the fourth attempt was worse — the focus point came from a path ENDPOINT that turned out not to be a grain at all. The grain detector for movie 2 misses this movie's grains entirely (nearest detected grain to a KNOWN grain: 130.8 px). RULE (H373): on this movie, never place a task's focus from a machine-trace endpoint, a vision read, or the m2-tuned grain detector — only human-placed or human-confirmed positions. The correct future construction for owned absence is the `owner` task type (the annotator marks the balls himself and presses No tube per row), whose new-owner linking to the absence loss still needs verifying before it is tried. |
| H374 | **snap25 built and verified; the round's material reaches the sample builder.** The rev10round project (3 emergence-stub body masks + 3 crossing annotations) is now a snapshot source. snap25: 124 obs, 15 body masks (12 + 3), 11 extent-usable, 9 crossings, and the sample census is 138 (path_tip 10, no_tube 33, tip_only 50, neg_region 16, body_mask 15, comparison 14) with the three stub masks present as body_mask samples at frames 28770/27720/26460, links explicit 8 (+3) and 4 quarantined as before. | H377 | **The interval deliverable ran with the retrained front: every event improved, and SELECTION is now the measured, isolated bottleneck.** `rev10_interval_snap25` (6 clump events, checkpoint best_front_ep19) vs the earlier pass with ep16, same scene and events — winner route-to-gold distance fell on ALL six: 37.5->6.1, 117.1->85.0, 113.2->107.3, 66.8->54.4, 5.4->1.6, 5.1->4.6 px. Availability is not the problem: the gold tip lies 0.01-1.46 px from some PROPOSED candidate's path in 6/6 events (`front_available: true` at a named 15 px tolerance), and the export now carries provenance (`{'proposed': 220}`, no oracle substitution tainting the pool). That is the next work item, and it is exactly the shape WP-C predicted would remain after the objective and architecture questions were settled. | H378 | **Selection dissected with every exposed signal: it cannot be fixed by re-ranking the current candidates, and that is the measured answer.** The exposed keys are now all real in the export (image support `ev`, front evidence `front_evidence{peak_logit,peak_prob,margin}`, `body_support`, `sel`, `route_p`) and were compared as rankers at equal data on the six clump events (winner route-to-gold distance per key, versus best-available 0.0-1.5 px). Findings: (1) `sel` == `front_peak_prob` x 0.5 exactly (route_p is a constant 0.5 here), i.e. selection ALREADY ranks by the front head's learned cap confidence — there is no unused learned signal hiding; (2) no single key ranks the correct candidate first: front_prob/margin/body/ev each win some events and lose others (ev x sel solves p02 at the best-available 1.5 px but wrecks p00 at 61.0); (3) the mechanistic detail: on the CLOSEST routes the front head's peak sits at 15-33% of the route (the proximal grain-exit junction) with LOW confidence (0.024-0.21), while its confident picks peak at 57-80% (0.10-0.28) — the head is keying on junction-like structure, not the distal cap, on these frames. Conclusion: the fix is NOT a re-ranker over current scores; the route-level evidence itself must become discriminative, which is a supervision task — and the banked crossing interval + emergence stubs are exactly the material for it. |
| H379 | **`ev` (frame-normalised image support) was silently zero on every exported candidate unless the evidence-GATE flag was passed.** `_ev_norm` was computed inside `if a.select_evidence_gate > 0.0:`, so runs without `--select-evidence-gate` exported `ev: 0.0` for all 220 candidates (H344 family: a measurement coupled to an unrelated consumer). Now computed unconditionally (one gradient + 95th-percentile per event); verified live: ev range 0.0-7.51 with 207/220 nonzero. This is the review's "expose all inputs in the exported record" made true rather than nominal. |

The factory now zeroes an absent head ONLY when the COMPLETE legacy key set is missing (partial heads refused), preserves every present parameter, validates model_schema/preprocessing against supported definitions (schema-999 and incompatible-preprocessing fixtures refused), and reports the POST-LOAD parameter hash in `info`. The runner defaults to the strict current-checkpoint path, records `front_load` provenance (mode, config hash, parameter hash, manifest), supports an explicitly requested two-stage legacy migration (own declared architecture first, CLI-declared second) and `--stop-after-load` for entry-point testing. The patch alone does NOT improve accuracy (0/6 remains). |

The band-selection step (`_licensed_band`) is shared by BCE and the balanced-Dice region: the audit's 908 unreviewed-band pixels with nonzero Dice gradient are now 0 (403+122+34+349 reproduced exactly, licensed-band and self-positive gradients intact — `scripts/rev11_gradient_probe.py`, pass=true), and a band without a reviewed-background license fails closed. `actual_contributions` moved AFTER resolved-prompt masking + weight gating, with opportunities/zeroed-no-query/nonzero categories and train/dev phases + pixel mass reported separately. 60-step fit probe reruns green (loss 8.36 -> 1.41, foreign term alive). |

Trainer (`sample_prompt`) now draws `auto-grain` discs from routes.auto_grain output on the query frame — the SAME construction as runtime (`deployment_prompt`); verified on real r4-p00 pixels: identical center (389.0,162.0), radius 6.0, source frst, tensor-equal masks, delta 0.0 px (the audit measured a 45-68 px trainer-vs-runtime gap; the focus for r4-p00 sits 46 px from the detected center). | H430 | **rev13 W3 contingency attempt 2 IMPLEMENTED: the encoder-fed scorer variant + the regression that would have caught H429.** `cap_window_encoder` is a declared architecture flag: when set, `score_cap_window` samples the encoder's per-frame feature map at the SAME oriented patch grid, pools it per location, and fuses it with the raw-patch features before the ordered temporal convolution (own module set cw_feat/cw_temporal_e/cw_logit_e/cw_dxy_e/cw_logvar_e, so raw-variant checkpoints keep loading strictly; the factory maps the flag from config). VERDICT: **fit-gate NOT passed, all three terms, with a DIFFERENT failure mode**: the encoder-fed scorer is no longer constant-absent — it confidently accepts 46 decoys while accepting ZERO true positives (tp=0/fp=46/tn=166/fn=23; accuracy 0.7064 BELOW the always-absent baseline 0.9021), and the per-row evidence (277 rows persisted, cap_max_prob for all 235 labeled) shows it ranks present rows BELOW absent ones: AUC(present>absent) = 0.1762 (present mean 0.364 vs absent 0.418) — an INVERTED scorer, not a blind one. Six dev tips remain 0/6 at top-1/3/5 (deployed-pool oracle 2/6; support reach 6/6). So: bounded arm 1 (frozen trunk, raw pixels) collapses to constant-absent; bounded arm 2 (adapted encoder, encoder-fed) inverts. Both terms are recorded; the next decision (different encoder, different supervision design, or annotation on these failure mechanisms) is the reviewer's, per the work order's decision tree. |
| H431 | **rev13 W3: the inversion DIAGNOSED to a border-clamp artifact, and W3.4's validity rule implemented.** Row-level analysis of the encoder-fed eval (`rev13_capwindow_enc_eval.rows.json`, argmax location per labeled row): 25% of ABSENT rows' argmax locations sit within 12 px of the crop border (p10 = -10.9 px — OUTSIDE the crop), where grid_sample clamps and smears the edge; 0% of present rows' argmax locations (and 0% of the 82 true-positive locations) are near the border. The scorer learned the clamp artifact, which is why it fired on decoys and never on caps (AUC 0.1762). Implemented exactly the W3.4 rule: `score_cap_window` now returns a per-location VALIDITY mask (a patch whose sample points leave the frame is invalid), training multiplies the supervised mask by it (invalid locations get NO label — never a silent negative; the same rule applied to the local arm), and eval excludes invalid locations from emission (sc -> -1) while recording the count. 8 cap-window tests green (validity mask test included). Corrected arm (encoder-fed + validity masking, same labels/contracts/losses/updates) chained: pre-flight -> fit -> eval -> gate -> AUC, landing in rev13_capwindow_v3*. VERDICT: gate NOT passed, but the fix moved the numbers in the predicted direction and the residual is now sharply defined. Decoy acceptance fell 46 -> 3 (the border-clamp artifact was indeed a major contributor), accuracy 0.8894 with tp=0/fp=3/tn=209/fn=23 — and the ranking is STILL inverted: AUC(present>absent) = 0.2069 (present mean 0.362 vs absent 0.400). So after removing the clamp artifact, the scorer still finds present-row patches LESS cap-like than decoy patches, and no location on any present row ever crosses 0.5. The residual inversion must be diagnosed WITHIN rows next: compare the scorer's per-location logits at the true positive locations against matched negatives on the same present rows (same image, same route) — if positives score below their own row's negatives, the evidence the scorer reads at the tip contradicts the labels, and the supervision design (label construction at the tip / the objective) is the next variable, not capacity or artifacts. WITHIN-ROW DIAGNOSTIC RUN (v3 rows): on the 23 present rows, max(cap_prob) at the true positive locations vs max at the same row's own negatives — 12/23 rows INVERTED, means 0.347 vs 0.358, i.e. COIN-FLIP separation within identical images and identical routes. The trained scorer carries no measurable location-specific cap evidence at this budget under this supervision; the three bounded arms (constant-absent, border-inverted, artifact-free-chance) close the W3 experiment line with every term recorded and the mechanism named. |

The within-row hinge did NOT produce threshold-crossing discrimination: cap confusion returned to constant-absent (tp=0/fp=0/tn=212/fn=23; accuracy 0.9021 = the always-absent baseline exactly), AUC(present>absent) = 0.2183 and the within-row inverted count stayed EXACTLY 12/23. The hinge gradient is level-independent and always pushes separation, yet 240 updates on the encoder-adapted arm still land on a threshold-crossing-free solution — so under this input representation (24px raw+encoder patches at route locations), this head and this budget, the cap evidence a human sees in ~2 px is not being extracted. FOUR bounded arms now agree: constant-absent (raw, BCE) -> inverted (encoder-fed) -> artifact-free chance (validity) -> constant-absent again (hinge). Every term recorded with its mechanism; the W3 bounded experiment line is CLOSED. Next variable is not capacity, not labels (certified, H433), not artifacts (removed, H431) and not this objective (tested, H436): it is the input representation / a genuinely microscopy-adapted encoder or a different supervision construction (e.g. patch-level positive mining around certified tips with hard negative banks) — a research decision for the reviewer per the work order's decision tree. |

| H485 | **Protrusion-growth emergence onset detector (WO3 replacement for the failed per-frame rim logit).** The rim-disruption logit was confounded: a slightly irregular grain rim ROTATING into view reads as "disruption" exactly like a tube, so its per-frame score on cf70 was noisy/non-monotonic (spurious early cluster f300-f1200, quiet f2700-f4200, rise f4500+, plus pathological -17 outliers) and it mis-ordered cf70 (f300 absent scored +1.76 ABOVE the real emerged f6000/9000). Onset = growth-knee with two physical guards (>=1.5px net growth; persists to end = a tube never retracts) + smoothed first-crossing. Validated on both reviewed sparse onsets: **cf70 onset 5100 vs human (300,6000] = in-bracket at the clean knee (flat 2.4px pore -> 5.5px tube); G0 onset 7300 vs human (8000,8150] = ~700 EARLY.** Honest limits: cf70's curve is clean (flat->rise) so its onset is tight; G0's curve is noisy with no clean baseline (rim fluctuates all through) so its onset is resolution/ambiguity-limited and no threshold lands it in-bracket without pushing cf70 to its bracket edge (frac sweep 0.4-0.6: cf70 5100/5100/6000 in-bracket, G0 7300/7600/7900 all early). A fixed-angle-tracking refinement (track the protrusion at one dominant tube angle) was tried and REVERTED — it mis-selected the tube angle (cf70 190deg vs its 10deg tube) and degraded both; per-frame-max is kept as measured-best. 4 tests `tests/test_rev16_emergence.py` (growing-bump knee onset, flat->none, single-spike->none, boundary-reach protrusion).
